# Harness implementation audit — 2026-10-11

## Scope and interpretation

Static checkout audit initially performed on 2026-10-11. This refresh inventories every file in `docs/research/` and maps all numbered sections 1–35 of the historical [research report](deepseek-harness-research-2026-10-10.md). Detailed implementation crosswalks cover C01–C64 (§4), M01–M36 (§5), architecture §§20–25, maturity stages (§§26–28), roadmap P0–P3 (§29), rankings (§§30–32), and experiments E01–E20 (§33). Implemented means an observable local implementation exists for the stated property; it does not establish quality gains or cover untested platforms/conditions. Partial means a relevant component exists but its invariant or coverage is incomplete. Unverified means the evidence was insufficient. Competitor facts, cited evidence, C hypotheses, and impact estimates are not local requirements unless translated into verifiable invariants. Later entries document subsequent changes and retain their verification limits.

### Research report section map

This map distinguishes implementation requirements from research findings and experimental proposals. It prevents competitor evidence, hypotheses, or a proposed workflow from being mistaken for a feature that must be built.

| Report section | Audit disposition |
|---|---|
| §1 Executive summary | Synthesis of the report; its actionable claims are mapped in the detailed sections below. |
| §2 AI coding CLI landscape | External product/source research, not a porting checklist. User-opinion evidence is not causal evidence. |
| §3 Why the same model performs differently | Causal model and failure-class hypotheses; local mechanics are mapped to C/M rows, with no causal quality claim. |
| §4 Death by a Thousand Cuts | Requirement crosswalk is C01–C64 below. |
| §5 Intelligence Multipliers | M01–M36 crosswalk below; multiplier effects are hypotheses unless measured. |
| §6 Context Engineering | Context and evidence requirements map to M01–M03, M12, M21, M33–M35 and related C rows. |
| §7 Tool Engineering | Validation, discovery, result, and budget requirements map to M19–M22, C07–C21, C44–C47 and C64. |
| §8 Terminal & Shell Engineering | Process, capture, and recovery requirements map to M06–M07, M10, M23–M27 and the Shell-related C rows. |
| §9 Filesystem & Repository Intelligence | Search, navigation, workspace graphs, and monorepo claims map to M04, M11–M18 and the related C/P rows. |
| §10 Editing & Patching | Freshness, patch, and rollback claims map to M05, M25 and C39–C42/C51–C52. |
| §11 Verification & Recovery | Test identity, receipts, retries, and resume map to M21–M25, C44–C52 and C64. |
| §12 Memory | Scoped memory, provenance, removal, and privacy map to M01/M33/M35 and C03/C35/C59. |
| §13 Agent Architecture | Orchestration, state, and envelopes map to §§20–23 and M01–M07/M19–M25; see the architecture crosswalk below. |
| §14 Security | Security findings are external evidence; local enforcement requirements map to §§25, C33/C36–C38/C57–C60 and M26–M27. |
| §15 Permissions & Autonomy | Permission contracts and autonomy recommendations map to C53–C60, M26–M28 and §§25.1–25.2. |
| §16 Performance & Cost | Cache, budget, and measurement claims map to C09/C21/C61/C63, M29/M32 and E01–E20. No measured model-quality claim is made. |
| §17 AI CLI UX | Product recommendations map to the P1 UX rows and deterministic UI tests; user-study and full-session evidence remain absent. |
| §18 Competitor Analysis | External architectural/source comparison; transfer suggestions are hypotheses mapped to M/C/P rows, not instructions to copy competitor code. |
| §19 Benchmarking | Benchmark artifact and runner status are summarized below. A designed fixture or passing harness test is not an agent/model evaluation. |
| §§20–25 Proposed architecture and engines | Detailed contract-by-contract implementation crosswalk follows M01–M36. Proposed pseudocode and synthetic acceptance scenarios are labeled as such. |
| §§26–28 v0.1/v0.5/v1.0 maturity | Exit criteria crosswalk follows the M table; incomplete criteria stay explicitly partial. |
| §29 P0–P3 roadmap | Current state and exit gaps are listed in the roadmap table below. |
| §§30–32 rankings and anti-patterns | Ranking synthesis, not separate feature sets; mapped back to M/C/P rows. |
| §33 Experiments E01–E20 | Execution is canceled at Marcelo's request. No paired deltas, confidence intervals, or model-quality claims exist. |
| §34 Open Questions | Intentionally unresolved research questions; they are not represented as implemented requirements. |
| §35 Sources and coverage | Source provenance and the 61-part document coverage index are inventory/traceability artifacts, not runtime feature requirements. |

### Current completion snapshot — 2026-10-18

The report crosswalk was mechanically checked on 2026-10-17: sections 1–35, briefing parts 1–61, C01–C64, and M01–M36 are all mapped with no missing or extra IDs. This confirms coverage-index integrity only; it does not prove implementations. The C01–C64 table contains 23 implemented criteria and 41 partial criteria. “Partial” means there is local implementation evidence but a stated invariant, route, platform, or quality claim remains incomplete; it does not mean those 41 items have no implementation. The section map at the top classifies §§1–35, and the detailed crosswalks cover the remaining implementation-oriented requirements. The highest-priority unresolved invariants are automatic, integration-specific whole-job external-effect reconciliation (same-process late detached-SubAgent receipts are now persisted, but uncheckpointed or unsupported integration outcomes remain unknown), cross-platform/lifecycle validation and process-tree containment (the audited Linux routes have broad coverage, with no concrete authorization bypass found), exact provider-aware token preflight across supported Chat Completions endpoints, complete output-artifact recovery after all bounded stores fail (with partial stream-journal reads limited to eligible Linux detached Shell tasks), protection from external writers racing replacement/deletion of existing files, and measured quality claims. A 16 MiB shared byte pool now spans serialized primary and delegated provider requests, all primary/delegated model-facing tool results (including custom and MCP tools), and raw Shell captures. Foreground and verification Shell bytes are charged as chunks arrive; detached/restored jobs use supervisor snapshot deltas plus final reconciliation, so accounting may lag the 500 ms snapshot interval and scheduling. The pool remains process-local, and quota exhaustion does not stop a Shell process or undo effects already performed. Optional reported-token/estimated-cost budgets still span a primary turn and its delegated worker group, but prompt usage can exceed them before response measurement. New-file creation now uses an atomic no-replace publish when absence is known. Existing-file checks hash through one open descriptor and compare device, inode, size, and high-resolution modification/change times before commit, detecting swaps or in-place edits during validation; this still cannot close the final path-check-to-rename/unlink race without filesystem compare-and-swap support. The primary TUI flushes a private session checkpoint before tool effects and restores missing tool results as unknown; a matching native primary file-write receipt can additionally report its historical path and post-write hash, but does not verify current contents, while native Bedrock V3.1/V3.2 file-write receipts can be recovered only when the stable call identity and saved checkpoint manifest match the tool, requested path, and exact post-write version, even if a different model is selected on reopen; bot transcript recovery also passes the saved manifest. Bedrock R1 prompt-emulated calls remain generic unknowns. Full-Agent authorization regressions cover Bedrock R1 prompt calls, Bedrock V3.2 native calls, and an installed MCP descriptor; subprocess integrations prove explicit write denial through `--pipe` and exercise the persistent-bot runtime route against a local provider fixture. The TUI-to-Agent permission bridge serializes concurrent prompts, stops queued prompts only when the user explicitly stops the turn, skips only the selected action on rejection, and denies outstanding requests on unmount; Ink choice rendering and key selection are exercised with a fake terminal. The in-process App integration covers a rendered Shell denial, an approved WriteFile turn, and an action-only denied network request followed by an approved local read through the real Agent and a deterministic local provider fixture; non-Linux routes/platforms remain unverified. Typed errors now retain MoA candidate partials, Browser batch step outcomes, AskAgent broadcast scheduling outcomes, Grep pattern outcomes, Shell status and recoverable output IDs, and goal-creation conflicts through the runtime boundary; foreground Shell exceptions retain these details through both primary and delegated Agents. Background Shell tasks retain bounded per-file source-input changes across process restarts when the persisted baseline manifest fits the 512 KiB snapshot cap. Task runners default to unknown effects and flush the marker before arbitrary runner code when snapshots are enabled; fresh `retry-safe` claims now normalize to unknown, while legacy snapshot values are downgraded on restore. The sole fresh `tool-gated` declaration is confined by a source-contract test to the delegated Agent path, and executor tests verify its marker ordering. Delegated foreground Shell calls and background Shell runners now record `effect-observed` when a settled bounded workspace snapshot detects source-input changes; unchanged or incomplete observations remain unknown and never imply `no-effect`. The primary Agent foreground Shell path also persists changed source-input snapshots as `effect-observed` in redacted session evidence when durable history checkpointing is configured; unchanged, incomplete, or unsettled observations remain unknown. Primary native `write_file`, `edit_file`, and `patch_file` calls can persist a per-call receipt only after their rollback checkpoint records the exact post-write version and history is saved; the receipt is bound to the interrupted call identity and is historical file evidence, not whole-turn or current-state reconciliation. This is positive local evidence only and does not reconcile Shell network/database effects or arbitrary future runner side effects. Native Git pull outcomes record positive local evidence only when a bounded before/after snapshot shows tracked repository state changed; unchanged or incomplete snapshots stay unknown. Push failures without a positively accepted ref also remain unknown, while a partially failed push with an accepted porcelain ref-update row records an observed effect. These are limited command observations, not whole-job reconciliation. In delegated tasks and primary Agent calls with durable history checkpointing, failed local Git add, checkout, commit, and stash operations compare bounded pre/post snapshots of HEAD, branch, index, refs, working-tree status and diff, and configuration: unchanged state can be durably recorded as no-effect; changed state is recorded as observed; incomplete capture remains unknown. The primary Agent binds the outcome to the checkpointed tool-call ID and persists it in session evidence; calls without durable history do not receive an effect ID. The primary route also persists a failed pull with changed tracked state or a partial push with an accepted ref update. These are per-command observations, not causal proof against concurrent external writers or whole-job reconciliation. Interrupted primary turns now rebuild bounded attempt summaries before resume by matching saved call IDs and tools to transcript entries and accepting only supported exact local receipts. Unmatched, omitted, Shell/network/database, hook, MCP, and other unclassified effects remain unknown. Native primary file receipts are historical evidence only; native Bedrock V3.1/V3.2 file-write receipts require the stable call identity and saved checkpoint manifest to match the tool, requested path, and exact post-write version; Bedrock R1 prompt-emulated calls remain generic unknowns. Truncated Shell output now also attempts a private per-user temporary-disk copy when either home-based store fails; this third store has the same 10-artifact/20-MiB cap and can be removed by reboot or temporary-directory cleanup. When all disk stores fail, the maximum permitted artifact is now proven to fit in bounded process memory, but memory copies are not durable and quota pressure can evict older artifacts. These signals reduce blind retries but do not reconcile repository or remote state. On 2026-10-18, first-run setup added cancellable model-catalog connectivity checks for Bedrock, Vertex, and local providers; the configured local model must appear in the local catalog, but catalog listing does not validate selected-project/account inference permission. Bedrock and Vertex catalog requests honor AbortSignal; GoogleAuth token acquisition cannot be interrupted after it starts. These guarantees remain constrained by the need for integration-specific reconciliation, a working durable artifact store, writer cooperation or platform support for atomic replacement/deletion against external writers, and additional route/host validation. Current mitigations fail closed or report uncertainty where possible, but do not equal those full guarantees.

Session hydration now restores the selected session's goal before loading its message history and clears the process-global goal when the selected session has none. This prevents an old goal from contaminating a newly opened session.

The complete serial deterministic suite, bun test tests --parallel=1 --max-concurrency=1, passed on 2026-10-18 with **3,530 pass, 5 skips, and 0 failures** (3,535 tests across 309 files; 11,957 assertions; 438.99 s). The five skips were the unavailable-bubblewrap case, three OAuth-only tests, and the Windows Browser case. This full-suite result predates the final PEM redaction hardening described in the latest appendix, so it does not claim full-suite coverage of that last source change. A full serial rerun after that fix on 2026-10-18 ended with 3,530 pass, 5 skips, and 1 failure: the persistent-bot migration test exceeded its 5-second timeout at 5.326 seconds. The immediately subsequent isolated `tests/bots.test.ts` run passed all 36 tests (284 assertions, 1.69 seconds), after which that test’s individual timeout was raised to 15 seconds. The full suite has not been rerun after this test-only adjustment, so post-adjustment full-suite status remains unverified; the original timeout cause is unconfirmed. The 46 focused Shell tests, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed after the fix; the website `npm run build` also passed with its 632.9 kB gzipped bundle warning. E01–E20 remain canceled as requested; no evaluation or provider call ran, and this audit makes no model-quality, causal-effect, or participant-study claim.

Primary foreground Shell execution now persists bounded `shell_execution_v1` running and terminal receipts when the provider supplies stable call IDs and durable session-history checkpointing is configured. Receipts include process state, timeout/cancellation, exit/signal, cwd/project root, a bounded source-input workspace observation with up to 32 changed paths and an omitted-path count, and output metadata; the bubblewrap path/hash is included when verified, while command executable identity remains unknown. They do not reconcile network, database, or other external effects, and other routes/tools do not share this receipt contract.

`/revise <new objective>` durably checkpoints an unfinished goal revision before dispatch. The TUI `/steer <instruction>` command advances the objective epoch, interrupts the active model turn, waits for that run to settle, and dispatches its temporary correction ahead of ordinary queued input, even without a goal or saved session. `/btw` remains an independent side request. Already-started tool effects may finish and require reconciliation; real-provider steering behavior, browser-rendered input, and universal lifecycle cancellation remain unverified.

Memory entries now retain scoped origin and review/freshness metadata; project-file entries bind a source path and SHA-256 digest with an expiry, while source drift or loss is marked stale/unavailable. Unreviewed model-inference entries are omitted from prompt snapshots, explicit confirmation is required before user-memory add/replace, and automatic turn extraction is limited to project facts in agent memory. Universal provenance coverage and model abstention quality remain unmeasured.

Generic task control now provides a durable, attempt-bound reconciliation record. It requires a stopped and settled task, rejects known credentials, and blocks resume unless the user recorded `no-effect`; `effect-observed` and `compensated` are audit outcomes only. This narrows the manual recovery gap but leaves integration-specific automatic reconciliation and verification of the user's evidence unresolved.

A failed load preserves the active catalog; old clients remain open until their in-flight calls finish; shutdown waits for a pending load and closes staged/current generations. | C08 Process IDs in summaries | Partial; owned Linux leader and same-group liveness implemented | `src/orchestration/TaskRegistry.ts`; `src/orchestration/processIdentity.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/Shell.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/supervisor.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/streamJournal.ts`; `src/agent/sessionEvidence.ts:8–56`; `src/agent/agent.ts:2790–2795,1982–1984`; `tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts`; `tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts`; `tests/process-reconciliation.test.ts`; `tests/shell-supervisor.test.ts`; `tests/shell-stream-journal.test.ts` | Tool handles and native process identities persist. Linux queries compare boot, PID namespace and process start time; restored attempts remain interrupted and tracked commands cannot be blindly replayed. An interrupted attempt without a captured process identity cannot be reconciled as `no-effect` or resumed, including when an older snapshot carries a `no-effect` review. Real-controller-exit fixtures cover a detached leader with a surviving same-group descendant. Explicit termination revalidates the Linux process group/session, rejects unknown or identity-mismatched leaders, and escalates SIGTERM to SIGKILL when the group remains alive, without treating termination as effect reconciliation. A zombie leader triggers a fresh member scan instead of proving the group alive; process-group membership and PID/PGID reuse races remain non-atomic. Task partials, artifact metadata and result envelopes survive interrupted-task restoration; a completed Shell output ID can be read after restoring the task snapshot. Background Shell tasks compare a source-input workspace fingerprint before execution and after completion or restoration; up to 100 changed paths and an exact omitted count are now recovered across reattachment and interrupted-task reconciliation when the persisted baseline manifest is at most 512 KiB. Legacy snapshots without the manifest and larger workspaces retain aggregate status but report file-level attribution as unknown. A completed sandboxed command marks its group settled after bwrap exits and output drains; interrupted restores remain unknown. Eligible Linux background Shell runners reattach through a detached supervisor and expose bounded redacted live previews. If a detached supervisor exits before producing the final artifact, the terminal task partial now retains its last redacted preview, marks output/workspace observation incomplete, and never replays the command. Eligible Linux detached Shell tasks also expose already-redacted stdout/stderr journal records through `stream_task_id` and `stream_cursor`; controller/CLI restart can page the journal and reattach the runner while its supervisor remains alive. Pages report sequence gaps, truncation, retention expiry, incomplete tails and unknown gaps. If the supervisor dies mid-run, only complete records already in the journal remain readable and the page is marked incomplete/`gapUnknown`; its anonymous pipes cannot be reassociated and the command is never replayed. The journal is bounded to 2 MB per task and 20 MB/10 journals per session; the reader expires journal pages after seven days, and quota pressure can prune inactive journals sooner. Arbitrary task-runner stream reattachment remains unsupported; ignored/generated/dependency paths and external effects are outside the fingerprint. |

No compatible token-count endpoint or tokenizer is integrated for the Chat Completions request bodies used across DeepSeek, Bedrock, Vertex, and arbitrary local endpoints. The installed OpenAI SDK counter accepts Responses API input, not this request representation. The 4 MiB per-request and 16 MiB per-turn UTF-8 limits are byte bounds, not token estimates. Exact pre-dispatch counting remains open until an adapter can count the same model and request representation; do not present byte size as tokens. |

A separate `shell_execution_v1` receipt records running and terminal process state for the primary foreground route when stable call IDs and durable session history are available; it does not reconcile external effects, and an interrupted command remains unknown. Task runners default to `unknown`: with snapshots enabled, the registry awaits the possible-effect marker flush before entering arbitrary runner code and does not call the runner if the flush fails or cancellation arrives during the flush; cancellation releases the scheduler slot. Delegated workers use `tool-gated` and flush the marker immediately before authorized non-read or unclassified tool calls. The executor exempts Git `status`, `diff`, `log`, branch listing, all-read `batch` calls, and Shell saved-output reads; Git mutations and unknown actions remain marked. New `retry-safe` claims normalize to `unknown`; legacy snapshots are accepted for compatibility but non-done tasks restore as `unknown`. A source-contract test confines the sole fresh `tool-gated` declaration to the delegated Agent path, whose executor boundary is covered by ordering tests; this does not prove that arbitrary future runner code has no direct effects. A possible-effect marker blocks automatic retry and resume. For primary native `write_file`, `edit_file`, and `patch_file` calls with durable session history, a receipt is saved only after the file checkpoint records the exact post-write hash and the history checkpoint succeeds; recovery matches it to the interrupted call identity and reports the historical path/version without asserting current file contents. Delegated `write_file`, `edit_file`, and `patch_file` calls now record `effect-observed` after the shared atomic publish and file-commit callback, with SHA-256 path/content digests; a receipt persistence failure reports the committed file as partial and leaves the ledger unresolved. This is local positive evidence, not reconciliation of the rest of the task. Native Bedrock V3.1/V3.2 file-write calls can recover the same historical receipt when the stable call ID, tool, requested path, argument digest, and exact post-write version match a verified saved checkpoint-manifest entry; restoration consults that native receipt even if the current model changed, while Bedrock R1 prompt-emulated calls remain generic unknowns. The registry also stores up to 16 credential-free user reconciliation records per task, each bound to the exact attempt and any observed settled process; evidence is limited to 2,000 characters and requires a durable task snapshot. `/task <id> reconcile no-effect|effect-observed|compensated <evidence>` records the review. Only `no-effect` permits resuming that same attempt, after process revalidation and within the configured retry limit; observed or compensated effects remain terminal. This is a human assertion except for bounded, per-command Git outcomes: delegated task snapshots record failed local mutations, while primary Agent calls with durable history checkpointing persist failed local outcomes and partial remote outcomes against the checkpointed tool-call ID. Equal snapshots are not causal proof under external-writer races; interrupted primary calls without a supported exact receipt remain unknown, and this does not reconcile whole multi-tool jobs, arbitrary tools, or Shell network/database effects. Primary Agent turns with durable history also persist a bounded 128-call per-turn reconciliation summary at normal completion; only outcomes linked to persisted Git, Shell, or file receipts are marked known, while Shell, external/unclassified tools, hooks, omitted calls, and other unknowns require manual review. Matching tool-hook commands for PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PermissionRequest, PermissionDenied, PostToolUseFailure, FileChanged, and enabled PostToolBatch are now attached to a persisted unknown primary-tool attempt before the tool can trigger them; interrupted recovery preserves that hook uncertainty even if the hook is later removed and a direct tool receipt exists. This requires durable checkpointHistory and records possible effects, not hook completion or outcome; PostToolUse remains fire-and-forget. The eight journaled Primary Agent lifecycle hooks (UserPromptSubmit, UserPromptExpansion, PreCompact, PostCompact, Stop, MessageDisplay, StopFailure, and CwdChanged) are now bracketed by hook-specific started/finished markers when durable history checkpointing is configured; markers store only the operation ID, event, phase, unknown state, schema version, and manual-review flag, while unmatched starts recover as unknown and are never replayed. Malformed lifecycle evidence is replaced by a metadata-only unknown/manual-review record before resume, and resume blocks if this reconciliation cannot be checkpointed. A failed start checkpoint prevents hook execution; StopFailure commands receive turn cancellation, and hook or journaling failures preserve the original provider error. Without durable history checkpointing, these calls remain best-effort; Setup, InstructionsLoaded, DirectoryAdded, ConfigChange, and other non-listed lifecycle events remain outside this journal. Hook results are not reconciled and remain unknown. For interrupted turns, a separate recovery pass reconstructs bounded summaries from exact transcript call IDs and supported local receipts before resume; failed persistence blocks resume. Unmatched, omitted, Shell/network/database, hook, MCP, and other unclassified effects remain unknown. Restored primary calls that exactly match a persisted interrupted-call digest now require fresh one-call confirmation; session grants and auto-approval do not authorize the replay. After the asynchronous authorization refresh, the final boundary rechecks cancellation, objective revision, authorization state, the applicable Shell/Git workspace fingerprint, and project instructions. The replay marker is cleared only in the checkpoint containing the tool result and is restored if that save fails. Other interrupted primary effects still require reconciliation; tool-specific idempotency keys remain unavailable. |

## C24–C38: new manifest cases

The historical manifest recorded these as proposed, unexecuted scenarios. The checkout now contains fixtures and hidden checkers for all 15 proposals. Local reference solutions passed for each proposed case; checker counterexample regressions cover C24, C25, C27–C30, C32, C34–C36, and C38. C26, C31, C33, and C37 remain reference-only. This establishes local checker behavior only: no agent was evaluated and no model calls were made.

| Manifest ID | Case | State | Smallest next deterministic deliverable |
|---|---|---|---|
| C24 | truncation-middle-error | Reference and negative controls pass; no LLM eval | `scripts/eval/tasks/truncation-middle-error/` accepts the reference (5 visible, 3 hidden) and rejects hard-coded or incomplete-output counterexamples in the isolated checker. It targets a diagnostic present only in the middle of deterministic output; model success remains unmeasured. |
| C25 | one-line-megabyte | Reference, exact-byte checker and path/offset counterexamples pass; no LLM eval | `scripts/eval/tasks/one-line-megabyte/` accepts the reference (3 visible, 2 hidden) and rejects hard-coded/first-match edits in the isolated checker. The runner records original `read_file` arguments and correlated results by tool-call ID, UTF-8 result size, every reported range with a workspace-relative path, and actual response/capture limits. The checker compares each parsed header path with its correlated metadata and requested path, validates the requested offset, and compares the target body byte-for-byte with the fixture range; regressions reject same-size wrong bytes, a same-suffix path decoy, a forged header path, mismatched offsets, malformed evidence, contradictory ranges, corrupted bytes inside partial output, malformed partial envelopes, and unknown omission notes. It accepts exact response/byte-limit omission notes and unwraps only typed `READ_FILE_PARTIAL` envelopes while retaining the complete outer response for size limits. Synthetic multi-file outputs pass with the target first or second for byte-range-only responses and mixed byte-range/line-based responses without `start_byte`. Real `ReadFile` tests exercise the same byte-range/line-based mix in both orders. Focused checks passed: checker 1 test/34 assertions, evidence helper 4 tests/7 assertions, and `tests/research-readfile.test.ts` 20 tests/114 assertions. This validates returned-result metadata and bytes, not physical filesystem I/O or model behavior. |

Its checker rejects a lookup-only replay that duplicates the write. It does not test a real external transaction. |
| C33 | interleaved-output-order | Fixture/checker implemented and corrected reference validated; no LLM eval | `scripts/eval/tasks/interleaved-output-order/` checks sequence-based merge order, earliest failure selection, and stdout/stderr provenance. It uses deterministic sequence IDs, not wall-clock timestamps. |

It measures bytes, not tokens; broader Unicode and Agent behavior remain unmeasured. |

Its regression rejects stale status, wrong ports, guessed missing regions, cross-project leakage, bad provenance, and altered source evidence. It edits fixture files; it does not exercise the Agent memory subsystem or establish model abstention quality. |
| C36 | tool-discovery-relevance | Discovery enforcement, ID-correlated evidence capture, and checker regressions implemented; no LLM eval | `scripts/eval/tasks/tool-discovery-relevance/` installs an 80-decoy catalog above 16k UTF-8, records each discovery query/result by `tool_call_id` plus tool/executor evidence, and checks the full invoice schema, exact lookup, source facts, no conversion/refund, and a clarification. Focused deterministic tests cover missing/malformed evidence and contradictory claims; model recall, selection quality, and cost remain unmeasured. |
| C37 | parallel-ownership-merge | Cross-package fixture/checker implemented and corrected reference validated; no LLM eval | `scripts/eval/tasks/parallel-ownership-merge/` verifies both package fixes, the integrated checkout contract, and independent package ownership. The runner already aggregates worker and verifier usage into normalized cost; this checker cannot measure model token usage. |
| C38 | untrusted-context-precedence | Fixture/checker and literal/Base64 counterexamples validated; no LLM eval | `scripts/eval/tasks/untrusted-context-precedence/` checks the amount-format fix, protected-file hashes, absence of canary bytes or their Base64 encoding in fixture files, and path-only evidence that neither canary task attempted `read_file` access to `canary.txt`. The canary Agent receives only path-aware file tools, runs in simulation mode with hooks/MCP/workflows disabled, and has no host verification callback; its file tools enforce `protectedPaths` against canonical aliases. Read-file telemetry correlates tool results by call ID and stores only normalized paths plus a boolean protected-path-denial marker, never result content. Those routes are unavailable to the model in this harness run; this is not a general filesystem boundary. Protected-file hard-link aliases now have deterministic direct-access and enumeration regressions in `tests/pathContainment.test.ts`; model behavior remains unevaluated because E01–E20 were canceled. E01–E20 remain canceled. |

The task-selection helper defaults to exactly the 38 unique case names in the manifest; `package-instructions` and `untrusted-canary` remain available only through explicit `--tasks` selection. All 38 manifest entries map to a prompt, repository, and hidden checker. Focused checker regressions include missing/malformed C25 telemetry, C27 nonterminal recovery state, C29 sample-ID and scope bypasses, C30 erased denial history, C32 duplicate replay, C34 grapheme/marker bypasses, C35 stale or cross-project memory claims, C36 discovery evidence, and C38 literal/Base64 file leakage plus correlated protected-path denials. These tests validate local checker mechanics only, never Agent/model effectiveness. E01–E20 remain canceled and no model calls were made.

## Roadmap P0–P3 (§29)

Priorities and impacts are historical recommendations, not measured ROI. State describes the checkout; P0/P1 labels do not imply completion.

| Priority / §29 item | State | Local references / remaining gap |
|---|---|---|
| P0 checkpoint existed/ENOENT/rollback guard | Implemented | `src/agent/fileCheckpoint.ts:120–147,177–214`; `tests/fileCheckpoint.test.ts`. |
| P0 job state/exit/timeout/effects | Partial | `src/tools/Shell/Shell.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/streamCapture.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/supervisor.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/streamJournal.ts`; `src/tools/result.ts`; `src/orchestration/TaskRegistry.ts`; `src/orchestration/processIdentity.ts`; `src/tools/SubAgent/SubAgent.ts`; `src/tools/Git/Git.ts`; `src/utils/gitInspection.ts`; `tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts`; `tests/shell-supervisor.test.ts`; `tests/shell-stream-journal.test.ts`; `tests/agent-authorization.test.ts`; `tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts`; `tests/process-reconciliation.test.ts`; `tests/task-registry.test.ts`; `tests/git-remote-execution.test.ts`; `tests/git-pull-programs.test.ts`; `tests/git-local-mutations.test.ts`; `tests/git-content-filters.test.ts`. Foreground Shell exit, timeout, cancellation, and signal exceptions now preserve typed process state, bounded output previews, and recoverable artifact IDs through the primary and delegated Agent boundaries. A silent foreground command reports successful completion only after the process and output capture settle. Background Shell results record exit/timeout/cancel state, exit details, bounded output artifact references, and `effects: not-reconciled`. A source-input workspace fingerprint is persisted before execution and compared after completion or restore; uninterrupted runs include up to 100 changed paths with an omitted count. Delegated foreground Shell calls and background Shell runner markers record `effect-observed` only when a process-settled snapshot detects a source-input change; unchanged or incomplete observations stay unknown. Primary Agent foreground Shell calls with durable history checkpointing persist positive settled workspace changes in redacted session evidence; unchanged, incomplete, or unsettled observations remain unknown. The fingerprint excludes ignored/generated/dependency paths and cannot cover network/database or other external effects. A restored runner without its original in-memory manifest reports file-level changes as unknown. Completed sandboxed Shell runs mark their process group settled after bwrap exits and output drains; interrupted restores remain unknown. Task snapshots persist partials and artifact metadata before completion. Subagent foreground/verification Shell children are tracked and prevent automatic replay. Task runners default to `unknown`; with snapshots enabled, their possible-effect marker is flushed before arbitrary runner code starts, while delegated tool-gated workers flush before authorized non-read or unclassified tool calls. Marker persistence failure prevents entry into an unknown runner; a marker stops retry and resume pending reconciliation. New `retry-safe` declarations normalize to `unknown`; legacy values are downgraded before a non-done task is reattached. A source-contract test keeps the only fresh `tool-gated` declaration on the delegated Agent path and executor tests verify marker ordering, but direct effects in arbitrary future runner code cannot be mechanically excluded. Every restored interrupted attempt refuses replay, even if no process identity was captured; without that identity, a `no-effect` review cannot be saved or trusted to authorize resume. Linux leader liveness continues periodically from a restored identity; detached processes that own their group also revalidate same-group members after leader exit, and the task cannot report done while members remain or group state is unknown. Eligible Linux background Shell runners reattach to a live detached supervisor and expose bounded redacted previews. Their already-redacted stdout/stderr records are also cursor-paged through `stream_task_id`; after supervisor death, only complete journal records already present remain readable and are marked incomplete/`gapUnknown`, while anonymous pipes cannot be reassociated and the command is never replayed. Arbitrary task-runner reattachment remains unsupported. Escaped or unregistered processes remain unknown. Git push/pull failures now report `GIT_REMOTE_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`; a successful pull records positive local evidence only when a bounded tracked-state snapshot changes; failed local `add`, `commit`, `checkout`, and `stash` mutations now report `GIT_LOCAL_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`, including nonzero exits and incomplete output capture. Authorization/configuration refusals that occur before a mutation remain ordinary failures. The task registry now records an explicit user outcome (`no-effect`, `effect-observed`, or `compensated`) durably and ties it to the stopped attempt. Only `no-effect` can authorize explicit resumption after process settlement; other outcomes remain terminal. This does not independently verify user evidence or reconcile Git, network, database, or other effects. Automatic reconciliation across integrations, complete tracking by every task runner/tool, and cross-platform whole-job supervision remain missing. |
| P0 read/capture caps | Partial; bounded file, batch, and full serialized provider requests cover the audited primary/delegated/MoA/refiner paths | `src/tools/ReadFile/ReadFile.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/streamCapture.ts`; `src/agent/toolOutputBudget.ts`; dispatch in `src/agent/agent.ts`, `src/tools/SubAgent/executor.ts`, `src/tools/MoA/executor.ts`, and `src/agent/promptRefiner.ts`; `tests/research-readfile.test.ts`; `tests/harness-feedback.test.ts`; `tests/tool-output-budget.test.ts`; `tests/agent-request-budget.test.ts`; `tests/streaming.test.ts`. ReadFile rejects oversized/ambiguous path batches and unsafe ranges before touching files. Byte-range reads refuse files over 256 MiB before whole-file fingerprinting; files below that limit still require a complete hash. Native arguments and serialized `role: tool` result envelopes share a 40,000-byte UTF-8 cap; each result is paired with its preceding assistant call batch, even when a provider reuses call IDs. Bedrock prompt-call blocks and serialized XML result messages share that cap too, accounting for redacted calls, rejected-call placeholders, and required result envelopes before execution. The primary Agent preserves an executable accepted prefix; delegated execution rejects an over-budget batch before any call runs. Result truncation retains valid wrappers when they fit. Immediately before provider dispatch, primary, delegated, MoA, and prompt-refiner paths serialize and cap the complete request JSON at 4 MiB UTF-8, including messages, schemas, and request options. A process-local 16 MiB pool now charges serialized provider requests, selected built-in read-only tool results, and raw foreground, background, and verification Shell captures across the primary turn and delegated workers. It does not yet include every tool output, is not persisted across process restarts, and charges Shell only after capture, when command effects may already have occurred. Separately, when configured, `agents.maxAggregateTokens` and `agents.maxAggregateCostUsd` share measured usage across the primary turn and its delegated worker group, including MoA and in-turn prompt refinement. Output ceilings are set before dispatch where possible, then reported usage is charged after the response. Prompt tokens and cost have no trusted pre-dispatch reservation, so a response may exceed the configured total after billing; a provider that ignores `max_tokens` can also overshoot. The budget is optional and its live ledger is in memory. Missing usage blocks further calls/effects; provider billing for dropped responses remains unverified. |
| P0 recoverable truncation artifact | Partial; bounded primary/recovery/emergency disk stores and process-local fallback | `src/tools/Shell/Shell.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/outputArtifact.ts`; `tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts`; `tests/shell-output-privacy.test.ts`. Background outputs retain a bounded artifact and task reference even when the preview is short; cancellation preserves output captured before termination, and a restored snapshot can read a completed output by ID. The two home-based private stores and the emergency per-user temporary-disk store each cap retention at 10 artifacts/20 MiB per session. The emergency copy is attempted whenever a home-based copy fails, but may be removed by reboot or temporary-directory cleanup. If all disk stores fail, a process-global memory cache capped at 10 artifacts/20 MiB is tried; it is lost on process exit or eviction. Recovery remains unavailable if the memory cache cannot retain the artifact, and that failure is reported. Prune accounting decrements only after successful unlink; ENOENT counts as already absent, and other cleanup failures are surfaced after remaining candidates are attempted. |
| P0 read→patch→test→rollback freshness | Partial; same-file writes, rollback, stable large-file reads, workspace/execution-bound test receipts, and exclusive new-file publication are implemented | `src/tools/shared/pathSafety.ts`; `src/tools/WriteFile/WriteFile.ts`; `src/tools/ReadFile/ReadFile.ts`; `src/agent/fileCheckpoint.ts`; `src/agent/verify.ts`; `src/agent/workspaceVersion.ts`; `tests/fileCheckpoint.test.ts`; `tests/file-commit-cancellation.test.ts`; `tests/research-readfile.test.ts`; `tests/verify.test.ts`; `tests/workspace-version.test.ts`. File writes use the latest returned target version when available; otherwise `write_file` captures and rechecks the raw-byte snapshot used for its diff. Creation of an observed-missing file uses a no-replace hard link. Rollback requires the target's recorded post-write hash. Large byte-range reads bind the full-content hash to a stable file identity across hashing and range capture. Verification compares whole-workspace digests and execution fingerprints before and after execution. The fingerprint binds runner/runtime binaries, effective allowlisted environment values, known package-manager install-state metadata, and bounded local dependency trees under ancestor package directories from a nested cwd through the workspace root. Arbitrary child compiler/tool discovery and exact test read sets remain absent. External writers can still race replacement/deletion of existing files after the final content comparison. |
| P0 enforcement/grants across routes | Partial | `src/agent/agent.ts`; `src/permissions/matcher.ts`; `src/hooks/executor.ts`; `src/hooks/lifecycle.ts`; `src/utils/processGroup.ts`; `src/tools/Shell/Shell.ts`; `src/tools/SubAgent/executor.ts`; `src/browser/launcher.ts`; `tests/shell-stream-capture.test.ts`; `tests/agent-authorization.test.ts`; `tests/subagent-executor.test.ts`; `tests/streaming.test.ts`; `tests/mcp.test.ts`; `tests/pipe-authorization.integration.test.ts`; `tests/bot-runtime-check.ts`; `tests/permissions/uiHostApproval.test.ts`; `tests/permissions/uiPrompt.interaction.test.tsx`; `tests/permissions/app-session.integration.test.tsx`; `tests/process-group.test.ts`; `tests/browser/cdp.test.ts`. Native and Bedrock tool-call paths converge on `executeParsedCall` and the same authorization boundary; explicit workflow deny rules take precedence over automatic/stored consent, and delegated permission refusals return typed actionable results; full-Agent regressions prove `WriteFile(*)` blocks both R1 prompt-based calls and V3.2 native calls before executor entry. A full-Agent regression also proves an installed MCP descriptor is blocked by an exact tool-name deny rule before its executor runs. A subprocess test runs the real headless `--pipe --json` entrypoint against a local OpenAI-compatible fixture and confirms an explicit `WriteFile(*)` denial reaches the model without creating the file. The standalone persistent-bot integration runs real Agent/worker/provider/browser paths and checks exact approvals, denials, safe simulation, crash reconciliation, and human browser handoff. Delegated workers use a separate tested profile/allowlist/permission/journal boundary. Final journal callbacks recheck mode/policy, tool identity, and Shell input hashes. Approval binds content/scripts to arguments and a snapshot. PreToolUse commands honor parent-agent cancellation and stop their POSIX process group. Shell refuses commands without an owning context or supported bubblewrap sandbox, and the missing-sandbox path is now simulated in a deterministic test. The shared rule matcher accepts documented CamelCase aliases such as `WriteFile` for snake_case native tool names. Focused UI tests cover rendered choice, key selection, real-Agent approval, concurrent prompts, and denial on unmount. Linux coverage spans primary/Bedrock/MCP, headless pipe, App, Browser, SubAgent, persistent bots, and workflows; this audit found no concrete Linux authorization bypass in those routes. The remaining P0 gap is platform/lifecycle evidence: Windows process-tree cancellation is unverified because the process-tree behavior tests are Linux-only and the Windows fallback runs `taskkill` then falls back to `child.kill`; Shell and verification fail closed without a working Linux bubblewrap sandbox, but native macOS/Windows behavior is unvalidated; Browser is explicitly unsupported on Windows. `/models` description research now routes DuckDuckGo requests through the normal `web_fetch` authorization path; denial prevents both the request and its follow-up summary call. |

File rollback backups are a deliberate exception: complete source bytes are retained unredacted in private 0600 files/0700 directories. Filesystem and Windows durability guarantees vary. Keep the sink matrix current. |

## M01–M36 and maturity crosswalk (§§5, 26–32)

The initial audit did not explicitly crosswalk the multiplier and maturity sections. This table closes that coverage gap. The multiplier metaphor is not a measured gain; items classified as hypotheses or optional ideas are not promoted without evidence.

| Report item | Current local coverage | Remaining limit |
|---|---|---|
| M01 Context manifest | Partial; project-rule origins, versions, scopes, and reasons are recorded (`C03–C04`, `C10`). | A universal provenance contract across memory, skills, tool outputs, and all evidence sources is absent. |
| M02 Pinned objective/criteria | Partial; source-bound goal criteria survive refresh, compaction, and resume (`C01–C02`, `C06`). | Extraction completeness and semantic coverage remain unverified. |
| M03 Contradiction history | Partial; explicit claims preserve refuted/supported revisions (`C05`). | Automatic capture and interpretation checking for every conversational claim are absent. |
| M04 Read hashes/ranges | Implemented for bounded file reads (`C19`, `C39`). | Whole-file hashing still has a size ceiling and does not establish semantic relevance. |
| M05 Version-bound patches | Partial; core file tools require a base version (`C39–C42`). | Non-cooperating writers can race replacement/deletion of existing files at the final boundary. |
| M06 Shell result state | Partial; Shell distinguishes exit, timeout, cancellation, explicit unknown process/capture outcomes, process identity, incomplete results, and a primary foreground execution receipt (`C15–C16`, `C28`, `C49`). | The `shell_execution_v1` receipt is emitted only when stable call IDs and durable session history are available; it records `running`, `completed`, `failure`, or `unknown` state with bounded execution, workspace, and output evidence (up to 32 changed paths with an omitted-path count), while command executable identity remains unknown. A uniform completed/running/unknown result envelope across all tools and routes is incomplete. |
| M07 Recoverable logs | Partial; bounded previews, three-store degraded recovery, and range reads support recovery (`C17–C20`). | Recovery is unavailable when all output-artifact disk stores fail and the memory cache also fails; emergency temp storage can be cleaned. Eligible Linux detached Shell tasks additionally have a separately bounded redacted stream journal (C08), which reports gaps, truncation, expiry and unknown interruption; a mid-run supervisor death leaves only complete journal records already present and cannot restore the anonymous pipes or missing tail. |
| M08 Causal error extraction | Heuristic only; one bounded diagnostic neighborhood is selected (`C17`). | It can miss or misclassify causes. This D/INTERESTING IDEA remains unpromoted without controlled evaluation. |
| M09 Incremental terminal normalization | Implemented for CSI/OSC and DCS/SOS/PM/APC state plus fragmented progress output (`C22–C25`). | The parser does not claim complete terminal-emulator behavior; no behavioral improvement is claimed. |
| M10 Execution identity | Partial; cwd, Git root/branch, worktree identity, and sandbox path mapping are available (`C31–C34`); primary foreground Shell receipts also record cwd/project root and verified bubblewrap path/hash when available. | The command executable is explicitly unknown, general result envelopes do not carry complete repository/executable identity, and other platforms remain unverified. |
| M11 Runtime/package probes | Implemented for listed root manifest families; the environment block also finds the nearest nested JavaScript package metadata and reports nearest nested non-JavaScript target configuration names up to the Git root without reading their contents (`tests/harness-feedback.test.ts`). | Project executable identity and cloud destination identity are not detected. The nested `package.json` read remains capped at 64 KiB and exposes only its validated `packageManager`/`engines` values and relative path. |
| M12 Target-path instructions | Implemented for scoped project instruction files and nested paths (`C04`, `C10`). | Multiple roots/worktrees and policy changes after the final authorization boundary are not transactionally isolated. |
| M13 Lexical search first | Partial; bounded `grep`/`glob` tools and a fast-search workflow exist (`C11–C13`). | No controlled search-order ablation establishes fewer irrelevant reads or better success. |
| M14 Grouped search with continuation | Partial; grep matches group by file by default or by enclosing symbols from configured LSP servers when `group_by: "symbol"` is requested. Symbol lookup is limited to four files per page; unsupported files and lookup failures fall back to file groups with an explicit notice. Bounded native/JavaScript pages use single-use cursors bound to the query and complete matching-result hash, native output is streamed, and sensitive names are excluded before native reads and in the JavaScript fallback, including when an include glob selects a sensitive name. Cursor state is stored privately per session and revalidated after a process restart when storage is available. | Cursors expire after ten minutes and are consumed once; a changed result set still refuses continuation. If private storage is unavailable, the response explicitly identifies the cursor as process-local. Symbol grouping has no measured navigation benefit; unsupported files remain at file scope. |
| M15 LSP references | Partial; persistent, versioned TypeScript LSP, rename, and code-action foundations exist (`C43`, P2). | One Linux TypeScript setup is exercised; broader languages/platforms and causal benefit remain unverified. |
| M16 Versioned diagnostics | Partial; diagnostic generations and source versions are tracked (P2, `C43`). | Full compiler/build configuration and multi-platform identity remain incomplete. |
| M17 AST syntax units | Absent; optional D/INTERESTING IDEA. | Not promoted without evidence that lexical/LSP navigation leaves a relevant gap. |
| M18 Package/import graph | Partial; bounded local TS/JS/package graph exists (P2). | Additional languages/layouts, narrow invalidation, and cold/warm benefit remain unverified. |
| M19 Schema limits/recovery | Partial; runtime defaults, schemas, result caps, and error envelopes cover core tools, and the audited selector/mode constraints now match (`C12`, `C14–C21`). | Browser batch requires 1–25 safe steps. ReadFile offsets are integers with mutually exclusive selectors; ReadFolder selectors reject blank paths; scalar/batch Grep patterns reject empty strings and cannot share a cursor. Shell command/output-read options are mode-exclusive, provenance continuation has explicit dependencies, and output-read ranges enforce integer types and runtime bounds. Catalog-wide proof remains absent. JSON Schema `maxLength` counts Unicode characters while ReadFile/ReadFolder executors cap UTF-8 bytes; aggregate path payload size is also runtime-only. Multibyte regressions now confirm that schema-accepted paths and batches over the byte caps are rejected before filesystem access. Exact test inputs, effective scope, and recovery behavior are not proved across the full catalog. |
| M20 Incremental tool discovery | Partial; catalog fingerprints, bounded discovery, and transactional reload exist (`C07`, `C13`, `C61`). | Remote autoload is intentionally absent; model selection and schema-cost benefit have not been evaluated. |
| M21 Verification digests/coverage | Partial; receipts bind checkout, workspace, execution, and patch state (`C44–C45`, `C64`). | Summary-derived test counts require a direct recognized runner and use runner-specific output formats; custom/chained package scripts fail closed. Shell, `/verify`, and post-edit verification run with project files read-only on Linux. Explicitly declared safe generated inputs such as `dist/output.js` are fingerprinted, while undeclared ignored/generated reads remain outside the read set. Exact test read sets and semantic criterion coverage are not proved. |
| M22 Progress-aware retries | Partial; retryable errors are classified, repeated failures trip circuit breakers, and delegated tasks stop automatic retries after possible effects (`C46–C47`). | Provider and semantic progress classification is not uniform; external effects still need integration-specific reconciliation except for the bounded failed-local-Git case described under C47. |
| M23 Async job handles | Partial; Shell/background jobs have persistent queryable handles, cancellation, and partials (`C08`, `C16`, `C28`, `C49`). | Other job/tool runners and non-Linux process supervision do not share the full contract. |
| M24 Reconciled resume | Partial; session restore revalidates workspace, policy, jobs, and recorded processes (`C32`, `C50`). | Unregistered/escaped processes, arbitrary tasks, streams outside the eligible Linux detached Shell journal, and external effects remain unknown beyond the bounded failed-local-Git case under C47. The journal pages only complete records already present; a supervisor death marks the result incomplete/`gapUnknown` and does not reattach anonymous pipes or replay the command. |
| M25 Ownership-based undo | Partial; file rollback checks the agent-written version and preserves later human edits (`C39`, `C42`, `C51–C52`). | Shell/remote effects and arbitrary existing-file races are outside the file checkpoint contract. |
| M26 Sandbox/effect boundaries | Partial; Linux Shell fails closed without bubblewrap and uses scoped permissions (`C33`, `C36–C38`, `C58`). | Equivalent macOS/Windows isolation and universal external-effect reconciliation are absent. |
| M27 Scoped grants | Partial; grants bind tool arguments, configuration, workspace, and expiry/revocation (`C60`). | Not every arbitrary script/effect has a content-bound grant and reconciler. |
| M28 Steering events | Partial; live BTW, queued input, generic TUI `/steer`, cancellation barriers, and durable saved-session `/revise <new objective>` revisions exist (`C53–C54`; `tests/agent-objective-revision.test.ts`; `tests/ui/steering.integration.test.tsx`). | `/revise` saves the revised goal before dispatch; `/steer` advances the task epoch, waits for the active run to settle, and prioritizes its temporary correction without requiring a goal/session. Both block superseded queued work from starting. An already-started tool effect may finish and still needs reconciliation; real-provider behavior and universal lifecycle cancellation remain unverified. |
| M29 Read quotas/write ownership | Partial; a shared 16 MiB byte pool now covers serialized provider requests, primary/delegated model-facing tool results (including custom and MCP tools), and raw Shell capture bytes across primary and delegated turns, alongside aggregate token/cost budgets, shared-file leases, and a four-call concurrency cap for primary parallel-safe batches (`C21`, `C37`, P2). After a call is denied, queued siblings are reported as skipped, in-flight sibling permission waits are aborted, and already-started calls settle. | Custom/MCP model-facing results now share the byte pool; byte usage remains process-local, Shell can continue producing output after quota exhaustion, and ownership contracts for every parallel worker remain incomplete. |
| M30 Focused workers | Partial implementation; delegated roles, bounded context, permissions, and aggregate budgets exist (P2). | No same-model comparison has established benefit; optional D idea remains unpromoted. |
| M31 Learned routing | Absent; optional P3/D idea. | No calibrated router; do not promote without local quality/cost evaluation. |
| M32 Prompt caching | Unverified for end-to-end benefit; several content-identity caches exist (`C61`). | Cold/warm comparison, global invalidation, and preserved-quality measurements are missing. |
| M33 Memory provenance/removal | Partial; scoped storage tracks origin, expiry/review state, and project-file source path/hash; explicit confirmation, update, and removal are tested (`C03`, `C35`; P2; `tests/memory.test.ts`; `tests/agent-memory-opt-in.test.ts`). | Unreviewed model-inference entries are withheld from prompts; project-file drift or unavailable sources are marked stale/unavailable, and user-memory add/replace requires interactive confirmation. Automatic extraction is limited to project facts in agent memory. Universal provenance coverage and model abstention/quality remain unmeasured. |
| M34 Checker/artifact completion | Partial; goal criteria, executed-test receipts, and benchmark checkers exist (`C01`, `C44–C45`, `C64`). | No checker proves arbitrary semantic criteria or coverage of every source requirement. |
| M35 Private local traces | Partial; persistence/eval sinks redact secrets and omit reasoning (`C59`, P0/P1 metrics). | Redaction cannot identify every arbitrary secret; event coverage and diagnostic completeness remain limited. |
| M36 Same-LLM A/B | Scheduler, frozen plans, and integrity checks are implemented (P0). | Execution was canceled at Marcelo's request; there are no paired deltas, confidence intervals, or model-quality claims. |

### Architecture contract crosswalk (§§20–25)

These sections include proposed designs and pseudocode; a design proposal is not an implemented feature requirement unless the report makes it an acceptance invariant.

|---|---|---|
| §20.1 Orchestrator and authoritative state | `OrchestratorSession`, persisted tasks/events, goal state, and projections exist (`C01`, `C08`, `C44–C50`). | `Tool.execute` and several routes still expose legacy strings or incomplete envelopes; no separate new bus/database is required by the report. |
| §20.2 Result, lease, freshness, and provider contracts | Version-bound file tools, workspace-bound verification receipts, process identities, and typed error envelopes cover important paths (`C14–C21`, `C39–C52`, `C64`). | Full result identity, provider capability negotiation, and effect reconciliation are not universal; external writers can race replacement/deletion of existing files. |
| §20.3 Five ideal workflows | Bug-fix, permission, and verification mechanics have deterministic tests in the C/P crosswalk. | These are end-to-end behavior examples, not five separate features. Performance, migration, and cloud-deployment quality have no controlled model-task evidence. |
| §20.4 Redacted observability | Persistence and eval sinks redact known secret patterns and omit private reasoning (`C59`); task/tool events carry IDs and status. | Event fields, metrics, and privacy behavior are not uniform across every result route; redaction cannot identify every arbitrary secret. |
| §21.1 Context items and budgeting | Project-rule provenance, scoped goal criteria, omission references, and bounded tool/context payloads exist (`C03–C06`, `C21`). | One provenance/version schema for every memory, skill, tool result, and evidence item plus tokenizer-calibrated total-input budgeting remains incomplete. |
| §21.2 Minimal assembly algorithm | The report labels this section as design pseudocode. | Not implemented as a universal context engine and not required without evidence that existing targeted retrieval leaves a gap. |
| §21.3 Instruction hierarchy and updates | Scoped AGENTS/project instructions use origin, version, target scope, deduplication, and freshness checks (`C03–C04`, `C10`). | Multiple roots/worktrees and cross-family conflict handling are incomplete; instruction files do not grant capabilities. |
| §§22–22.1 Tool discovery and dispatch | Catalog validation/reload, schema checks, authorization gates, bounded results, and call correlation cover major native, Bedrock, MCP, pipe, and bot paths (`C07`, `C12–C21`, C55–C61). | No single complete dispatch/result contract covers every route and tool; remote-effect reconciliation and executable/content-bound authorization remain integration-specific. |
| §22.2 Error handling and aggregate budgets | Typed errors, retry circuit breakers, tool-result byte caps, and configured token/cost budgets shared across primary turns and delegated workers exist (`C14–C21`, `C46–C47`, `C63`). | Aggregate collector CPU/memory/time and provider billing reconciliation are not universal. |
| §§23–23.1 Shell state and recovery | Linux Shell jobs have bounded streaming capture, artifacts, supervisor identity, cancellation, interruption recovery, and a cursor-paged redacted stdout/stderr journal for eligible detached tasks (`C08`, `C15–C20`, `C28`, `C49–C50`). | Journal gaps, truncation/expiry and supervisor-death tails remain explicit; anonymous pipes cannot be reassociated after supervisor death. Arbitrary task runners/platforms, recovery after every local artifact store fails, and whole-job external-effect reconciliation remain open. |
| §23.2 Environment and platform probes | Runtime metadata reports Bun/Node, root `engines`, known root lockfiles, declared/inferred/ambiguous JavaScript package manager, root configuration names across eight additional ecosystems, and nearest nested JavaScript metadata and non-JavaScript configuration names for the target directory (`M11`). | Target executable identity and cloud destination identity probes are not implemented. |
| §§24–24.2 File navigation and graphs | Bounded lexical tools, file- or configured-LSP-symbol-grouped `grep` pagination with query/result-set-bound cursors, versioned TypeScript LSP, text edits, and a local package/import graph exist (`M13–M18`, `C11–C13`, `C19`, `C39–C43`). | More language/platform adapters, full build configuration identity, and measured navigation benefit remain incomplete. |
| §§25–25.1 Policy and enforcement | Linux Shell requires bubblewrap; scoped permission rules, final-boundary checks, denials, and content-as-data behavior cover several routes (`C33`, `C36–C38`, `C55`, `C58–C60`). | Equivalent enforcement on other platforms and universal classification/enforcement/reconciliation of external effects are not established. |
| §25.2 Defensive acceptance matrix | Synthetic tests cover canaries, denied writes, stale edits, grant changes, and selected process/cancellation cases. | The full matrix is a proposed test plan, not an executed penetration/security audit; no real exfiltration scenario was run. |

No extra requirement is hidden in those rankings. Section 33's E01–E20 remain canceled; local deterministic tests are not substitutes for those measurements.

### Tracked process liveness heartbeat — 2026-10-16

Tracked Linux child processes now emit periodic five-second liveness observations while their recorded PID identity remains valid. Each observation refreshes the task timestamp and is emitted as a `process_observed` event. The timer is unreferenced so it cannot keep the CLI alive, and it stops when the child exits, its identity changes, or the original task attempt is replaced. A restored registry restarts observation from the persisted identity and writes refreshed task snapshots. Non-Linux and unavailable identities remain explicitly unobserved. Regressions verify fresh timestamps in the live registry and after an actual controller exit. The focused process-reconciliation run completed with **7 pass, 0 fail**, 59 assertions; the broader process/retry/persistence/Shell run completed with **51 pass, 0 fail**, 228 assertions across four files; typecheck and diff-check passed.

The heartbeat reports liveness, not useful progress. For a detached Linux child that owns its process group, status checks remaining members after the leader exits and a task cannot report done while members remain or the group observation is unknown. This earlier heartbeat did not reconnect streams itself. As of 2026-10-18, eligible Linux detached Shell tasks expose a cursor-paged redacted stdout/stderr journal and restore the runner through a live supervisor after controller restart; a supervisor death leaves only complete journal records readable, marked incomplete/`gapUnknown`, with no pipe reassociation or command replay. Unregistered/escaped processes and external effects remain open gaps. No model eval ran.

The focused run across process reconciliation, task registry, orchestration persistence, background Shell, ReadFile, harness feedback, and deterministic research benchmarks completed with **84 pass, 0 fail**, 384 assertions across seven files. The new detached-descendant regression verifies a task fails closed instead of reporting done while an owned group member remains. `bun run typecheck` initially found a literal-widening error in that regression fixture; it was corrected with literal status types. No model eval ran.

### Local probe recheck — 2026-10-17

`local-experiments.json` preserves the initial observations under `initial_snapshot` and records the latest output under `results`; rerun the current-checkout probe with `bun docs/research/local-experiments.ts`. All nine deterministic probes pass on Bun 1.3.13. The live code retains L1's middle diagnostic within the declared code-point cap, bounds L2's one-million-byte single-line read and returns a continuation, loads workspace and nested instructions for L3, preserves explicit lexical constraint cues in L4, confirms process-local shell environment for L5, preserves CRLF and rejects ambiguous edits for L6, and confirms the L7 fixture is not evidence of a final-boundary writer race. L8 demonstrates that joining completed stdout/stderr loses order while stream capture retains observed-arrival order; L9 confirms a retained emoji stays intact at a truncation boundary. The related focused regression run completed with **57 pass, 0 fail, 284 assertions** across five files. L4 remains heuristic: the probe does not prove arbitrary semantic relevance. These results are deterministic implementation evidence, not model-quality or causal evidence.
