## Integrity and conclusions

The current evaluation protocol is not evidence of effectiveness. `scripts/eval/run.ts:202–231,317–380` records initial-tree, prompt/grader/schema/runtime/config fingerprints; visible/hidden checks; per-checker-phase before/after hashes and the copied grader hash; integrity; optional transcript sanitization; estimated usage/costs; and `modelSeedSupported: false`. `scripts/eval/compare.ts:115–143,222–247` validates records, coverage, and configuration, blocking promotion when integrity fails. `scripts/eval/ab.ts` and `scripts/eval/schedule.ts` create/revalidate randomized arm plans and execute only with explicit `--execute`. This is instrumentation: it does not establish independent fixture/checker review or an actual A/B run.

Earlier isolated suite: 2460 pass, 4 skip. A subsequent run had 2476 pass, 4 skip, 2 fail: redaction performance and an obsolete preview expectation. Both were corrected and checked with focused tests. The next isolated suite finished with **2491 pass, 4 skip, 0 fail**, 6959 assertions across 224 files (171.90 s). That snapshot predates the Shell namespace correction under C31. Later checks: authorization/verification/checker/subagents — 58 pass, 184 assertions; Shell namespace — 3 pass, including explicit credential refusal. Typecheck also passed after the namespace fix, as did `git diff --check`. Affected C31 callers: 64 pass, 0 fail, 225 assertions across six files in an isolated copy; the later namespace test also passed the specific credential message. These focused runs cover the added invariants, not effectiveness. No LLM A/B ran; E01–E20 have no causal evidence.

### Actionable gaps by priority

- **P0, execution removed by Marcelo:** A/B remains unexecuted. Do not claim causal gains without the complete matrix, configuration identity, budgets, and frozen checks. No verified provider seed is available.
- **P0:** A static pass over all 28 executable checker files and their task prompts/visible-test counterparts is complete. Seven test-presence checks now share a TypeScript AST parser that recognizes callbacks registered through imported, non-skipped `bun:test` test functions and active suites; comments and literal payloads are blanked before structural checks, while semver and priority checks inspect specific AST literal values. Regressions reject fake calls in uninvoked functions, dead branches, and other test bodies. This proves static structure, not semantic coverage; behavioral targets and fixtures were not relaxed. A separate independent review and causal A/B evidence remain outstanding. Marcelo canceled model eval execution; local fixtures/tests are not an executed benchmark.
- **P0:** The Shell boundary is now explicit: only contextual execution with a working Linux bubblewrap sandbox can start; missing context or sandbox is refused before spawning. Regressions verify both no-context and missing-bubblewrap refusals without allowing the command to write a marker; the latter uses an isolated empty `PATH` and reset binary cache. Cross-platform process-tree behavior still needs host validation. Contextual Linux workspaces accept host paths and translate /mnt in file tools. Never call .deepseekignore a sandbox.
- **P1:** External permission, risk, workflow, and authorization-hook settings are reloaded before tool authorization and at the final executor boundary; standalone workflow starts recheck after consent as well. Changed policy revokes session approvals, updates orchestrator/workflow configuration, and cancels this agent's active delegated tasks and workflows; malformed settings fail closed. Detection is boundary-triggered, an external writer can still edit after the final reread, and already-performed or escaped external effects cannot be rolled back by cancellation. Tool-bound authorization decisions correlate to tool/denial evidence by `toolCallId`; a denied action survives compaction and resume, and fresh consent is required before retry.
- **P1:** Extend reattachment and supervision beyond eligible Linux background Shell tasks. Their detached supervisor restores the runner and bounded redacted previews; a separate cursor-paged journal exposes already-redacted stdout/stderr. If that supervisor dies, only complete records already present can be read, pages flag incomplete/`gapUnknown`, anonymous pipes cannot be reassociated, and the command is never replayed. Arbitrary task runners, escaped/unregistered process discovery, and external-effect reconciliation remain open.
- **P1:** Add uniform result envelopes and relevant execution/configuration fingerprints across every remaining route. Memory mutation failures now use stable codes; other composite outputs and tool-specific failures still lack universal coverage. Native and Bedrock tool-call/result batches share a 40 KB byte cap; the four audited completion routes cap the complete serialized request at 4 MiB UTF-8. Primary Agent, SubAgent, prompt-refiner, and MoA outputs also have finite byte caps, and non-DeepSeek primary calls request a finite default output-token value. Provider usage accounting remains outside these limits, and client-side response caps cannot prevent allocation of an oversized non-streaming provider response before it arrives.
- **P1:** Validate first-run options, clipboard/SSH, and metrics through integration tests. The wizard does not yet run readiness checks for every provider or expose workspace/runtime diagnostics; clipboard paste/selection round trips remain only partly covered; OS/tmux/SSH clipboard delivery and user comprehension still need host/participant validation.
- **P2:** Measure C36/E07 discovery recall, selection, and cost on paired tasks. LSP/CST/import graphs and caches follow matched-difficulty, matched-budget baselines with tested invalidation.

### Checker containment and subagent routes

`runIsolatedChecker` requires Linux with working bubblewrap namespaces before any model worker, using scheduler preflight as well. It mounts the fixture at /mnt, dependencies/runtime read-only, Bun as a single file, and private disposable HOME/TMP. It does not mount the host HOME. Network access is disabled except for explicitly marked browser fixtures. Frozen hidden-check directories are nested read-only mounts while the task workspace remains writable; persistent checker changes are also detected by before/after tree fingerprints. This is not universal containment of project verification or every platform.

### Frozen tool-discovery fixture and read-only hidden checker — 2026-10-17

C36 now has a deterministic fixture path. The harness installs the same oversized fake MCP catalog used to compute the frozen catalog fingerprint, then records discovery queries/results correlated by tool-call ID, tool calls, and executor-side effects for the hidden checker. The checker accepts one correctly typed invoice lookup, a positively phrased paid status, the USD 125.5 source total, and a currency clarification, while rejecting conversion/refund calls and a refund side effect. Reference-check regressions cover missing/malformed discovery evidence, a false status/wrong total, and a refund counterexample; this is not a model run.

The hidden grader copy is bind-mounted read-only during its sandboxed execution, while the task workspace remains writable. This closes transient checker self-modification during a run in addition to the existing persistent before/after tree checks. `bun test tests/eval-tool-discovery.test.ts tests/eval-checker.test.ts tests/eval-compare.test.ts` completed with **24 pass, 0 fail**, 115 assertions. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran. Causal discovery quality, recall, selection, and cost remain unmeasured because Marcelo canceled model evaluation.

`src/tools/SubAgent/executor.ts` uses evidence scoped to agent/session, allowlist-bound discovery, and redaction before the serialized limit. Provider copies and events are redacted. Subagent-ledger persistence across process restarts remains unproven.

### Mirrored Shell artifact integrity — 2026-10-17

Successful Shell artifact writes now persist a second redacted copy in the recovery store, and readers verify the SHA-256 checksum before accepting either disk copy. A damaged primary falls back to the recovery copy; if both copies fail integrity checks, the read returns an explicit integrity error. The checksum detects accidental corruption, not deliberate replacement by a writer who can recompute it. The selected recovery root is threaded through foreground reads, detached supervisor writes, and restored background-task reattachment. Tests also keep injected recovery storage inside ignored test state so it does not appear as a source-workspace change. Focused verification: **42 pass, 0 fail, 202 assertions** across four Shell artifact/capture/supervisor/background test files; `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. This does not make recovery durable if both disk stores fail and the process-local memory fallback is lost; artifact retention is up to 20 MiB per disk directory (40 MiB combined per session).

### Line-ending fidelity (C41)

Two normalization errors were fixed: PatchFile treated mixed-EOL snippets as uniformly CRLF, and EditFile inserted LF into a CRLF line. Both preserve unedited portions and report transformations applied to new content. PatchFile also rejects empty old_content before writing. Patch, line-edit, and diff-parser tests: **29 pass, 0 fail, 60 assertions**. Expanded isolated checks including authorization, subagent execution, and deterministic research contracts: **79 pass, 0 fail, 231 assertions** across six files. Typecheck and diff-check passed. The earlier complete suite of 2491 passes predates this correction and does not include these seven additional tests.

### Memory: removal, unique matching, and privacy

The memory schema already required a unique substring, but MemoryStore changed the first ambiguous match. Replace/remove now refuse empty or multiple matches without writing. Disabled memory is not mutated; removing/clearing one project does not affect another, and removed entries do not reappear in load/snapshot. MemoryStore redacts secrets before add/replace, when loading context, and in the migrated copy of the legacy layout. It uses a private temporary file (0600), atomic rename, and writer leases. Migration rechecks destination existence under the lease. The original legacy file is preserved; no claim is made that old secrets were erased from disk.

Memory tests: **25 pass, 0 fail, 63 assertions**. Deterministic research contracts plus subagent execution in an isolated copy: **66 pass, 0 fail, 198 assertions** across three files. Memory/persistence/workspace/orchestration integration: **33 pass, 0 fail, 113 assertions** across four files. Typecheck and diff-check passed. There is no per-fact source/validity metadata or demonstrated model abstention. Treating memory as a fallible reference without freshness guarantees does not replace those properties.

### Shell artifacts: secret masking and offset preservation

Recoverable Shell artifacts were private (0600) but still saved captured content without redaction. `src/orchestration/events.ts` now shares existing detection with maskSecretsForOffsets, replacing sensitive-value characters with * while preserving UTF-16 length, segment offsets, and CR/LF boundaries. saveShellOutput masks before truncation/persistence and records redacted. Reading/provenance disclose masked content. Legacy artifact reads mask the entire content before range selection, preventing leaks when start_char begins inside a secret. The old on-disk file is unchanged; no retroactive cleanup is claimed. Private-key blocks cut off at the capture boundary are also redacted through EOF.

Focused artifact/privacy/capture tests: **20 pass, 0 fail, 106 assertions**. They cover secrets split across segments/channels, preserved offsets/provenance, CRLF/emoji, legacy reads starting inside a secret, and incomplete private keys. Typecheck and diff-check passed. The first expanded run had 2509 pass, 4 skip, 3 fail: two tests used /mnt/x as an external path although /mnt is now a workspace alias; a bot test exceeded 5 s. The first two passed after using a genuinely external host path, preserving refusal assertions. The bot test passed alone in 160 ms without a changed limit. Focus on those two files: 54 pass, 0 fail, 347 assertions. The complete repetition finished with **2512 pass, 4 skip, 0 fail**, 7045 assertions across 226 files (169.45 s), without model/eval execution. This run predates the later goal-acceptance changes below. No pattern heuristic guarantees detection of every secret.

### Goal acceptance: original sources and current evidence

Agent.verifyGoalCompletion now gives the reviewer all redacted SessionEvidence request/denial sources separately from recent conversation and fingerprints the current workspace. If the full set exceeds 40KB UTF-8, review is refused rather than hiding requirements. Historical verification receipts are paginated and disclose omitted content; they do not automatically prove current state. After the response, runtime revalidates workspace, goal, sources, and receipt identity, rejecting approval if any changed. Errors/cancellation, unsafe fingerprints, and oversized sources leave the goal active.

Tests using a simulated model client: **17 pass, 0 fail, 83 assertions** across three files. They cover post-compaction sources, redacted canaries, human edits during review, new requests, receipt changes, oversized sources, and external symlinks. Typecheck and diff-check passed. The complete 2512-pass suite predates this change. No real model call or reviewer-semantic-quality guarantee exists. Typed criteria and per-requirement external checkers are still missing; hierarchical/paginated review above 40KB has not been implemented.

### Catalog integrity and schema validity at execution

validateSchema compiles immutable snapshots and invalidates its cache by content. The tool wrapper also invalidates closed schemas, removing previous Ajv registrations when the same $id changes content. Installation rejects duplicate names before replacing the catalog. At effects, the primary agent and delegated executor reject schema, description, or executor-function changes occurring during authorization/journaling. The primary agent reuses its existing validation, excluding injected internal fields such as __planFilePath; subagents revalidate arguments before calling the executor.

Isolated focus: **74 pass, 0 fail, 270 assertions** across six files, covering in-place mutations, IDs, duplicate catalogs, primary/delegated authorization, write_plan, and goal acceptance. Typecheck and diff-check passed. MCP/persistence/orchestration integration: **57 pass, 0 fail, 135 assertions** across four files. The previous complete suite does not include these changes. No real model/eval ran; the user canceled that step. Uniform semantic contracts and fully transactional MCP reconnection remain pending.

### Old sources after compaction and context omissions

Context now reports how many constraint previews and tool references were omitted, retains up to eight recent references, and points to the complete paginated index. Tool-only sessions also expose recovery. Micro-compaction queries the runtime ledger by tool name/call ID, preserving recovery references for JSON results without an embedded marker. It replaces results only when previews are smaller and estimates savings from characters actually removed.

An integration test executes ReadFile, compaction, snapshotting, and resume in another Agent with a deliberately incorrect summary. It recovers the old read through session_evidence, re-reads after editing the file, and shows historical sources remaining separate from current state. Expanded focus: **74 pass, 0 fail, 266 assertions** across six files; typecheck and diff-check passed. The compaction client is simulated; this does not establish that a model will always choose the relevant source. C02 dependency prioritization and C62 semantic fidelity remain partial.

### AGENTS revalidation during authorization and execution — 2026-10-15

AGENTS.md could change after the initial check, during consent or journaling. The agent now compares applicable content before preparing checkpoint/undo and again in the callback that would initiate the write. Changes, creation, or deletion refuse execution and request a retry to refresh context. Regression tests cover all three journal cases, zero executor calls, an absent destination, and clean undo state. This is a freshness barrier, not a transactional lock preventing external edits after the final check. At this stage, steering/DEEPSEEK.md did not share that invalidation lifecycle; the later project-rule manifest section records their integration.

The Linux environment message was also aligned with the implemented namespace: sandboxed commands can access /mnt and the absolute host workspace path, and contextual file tools accept both with containment/symlink/secret validation. The obsolete instruction forbidding /mnt was removed. A resumed complete execution could not be recovered after the environment changed: both handle and temporary log were absent, so it is not recorded as successful. An initial new copy without .git caused precondition failures in tests requiring Git, plus timeouts during simultaneous suites. These required repetition with corrected setup.

With Git initialized and no competing suite, authorization/instructions/compaction/completion/environment focus passed: **37 pass, 0 fail, 157 assertions**. The earlier complete execution had **2524 pass, 4 skip, 6 fail**, 7115 assertions across 229 files; those six failures are not counted as successful in that run. Git failures and timeouts in those five files passed under corrected focused setup without increased limits.

### Status-line extension cancellation — 2026-10-15

The sixth failure exposed a real defect: an external setsid wrapper can separate the supervised PID from the group that must be signaled. The status line now uses Bun.spawn with detached on POSIX, making the child its group leader. Timeout sends SIGKILL to commands/descendants ignoring SIGTERM. It retains the trust gate, scrubbed environment, output cap, and refusal of partial responses. Tests cover a descendant holding stdout open, a SIGTERM trap, and absence of a late file effect after timeout. Process-group behavior was exercised on current Linux/Bun; it does not prove Windows descendant containment or availability on older Bun versions.

Final status-line focus: **20 pass, 0 fail, 46 assertions**. Original timeout cases returned in approximately 34 ms. The SIGTERM-ignoring descendant test also waited through the late-effect window and confirmed that the file never appeared. No timeout was increased or assertion removed. Typecheck and diff-check passed after the runtime changes.

Complete repetition with Git and all changes in that block: **2528 pass, 4 skip, 4 fail, 1 error**, 7138 assertions across 229 files (352.53 s). Failures were 5 s timeouts in fences-send, a contended bot-write cancellation, askBtw, and diff review; the asynchronous error was Writer exited before its lock. AGENTS barriers, compact/resume recovery, namespace, and status-line tests passed. This is not a green suite and does not establish integrated stability. Limits/assertions were retained; focused repetitions are separate evidence.

Repeating the three affected files without another suite: **75 pass, 2 fail, 1 error**, 321 assertions (48.13 s). askBtw and diff review passed. In bot-controls, fences-remember and persist-before-procedure-rejection exceeded 5 s, followed by Writer exited before its lock. Therefore not every failure can be attributed solely to competing suites. Bot setup/lock/latency stability remained pending; tests and criteria were not relaxed to get green results.

### Contended bot writes without blocking cancellation — 2026-10-15

BotStore.writeOwned could honor a synchronous busy_timeout configured on its connection before returning to asynchronous retry. This blocked the event loop and delayed abort delivery/lease observation. Every synchronous attempt now uses busy_timeout=0 and restores the previous value in finally, including success and permanent errors. The transaction is still acquired before ownership checks; only database operations are retried, never external effects.

The regression ran against the previous implementation in an isolated copy: cancellation took **1009 ms** with a 1000 ms busy_timeout and failed the unchanged 500 ms bound. The corrected scenario passed without mutation, with the writer still holding its lock and settings restored. The test also covers operation success and error paths.

bot-controls contention fixtures replaced IPC handshakes and clock-based automatic release with filesystem barriers. The child signals after BEGIN IMMEDIATE and releases only after the parent demonstrates waiting/cancellation. They reuse waitForChildFile, preserving ownership, absence-of-effects, exactly-once, procedure/routine-state, and time-limit assertions. Eval fixtures/arms are unchanged. This removes dependence on IPC delivery without hiding a real lock failure.

Initial final focus: **82 pass, 0 fail, 515 assertions**, four files, 8.10 s (bot-controls, bots, kernel/store, and cancellation regression). The preceding complete suite remains historical evidence with four timeouts and an error; it was not repeated after this change. Focus does not prove all intermittent migration/IO latency resolved or every synchronous runtime path cancelable.

The barrier also rechecks the signal after synchronous work and before commit: cancellation triggered inside a callback rolls back the note written in the transaction. Regression checks rollback, error propagation, and busy_timeout restoration. Final repetition including this barrier: **82 pass, 0 fail, 518 assertions**, four files, 13.13 s; typecheck and diff-check passed. No new complete suite or model eval was run.

### Shared project-rule provenance and freshness — 2026-10-15

The primary agent now loads AGENTS.md, steering, and both DEEPSEEK.md locations into one project-rule manifest. Each record identifies canonical origin, directory scope, content hash, and sources/reasons for inclusion. Aliases of the same canonical origin and scope share one body. Equal text from distinct origins remains separate; different scopes remain separate. The manifest declares scoped AGENTS precedence, user/system precedence, and unresolved conflicts between rule families rather than treating presentation order as authority.

All three rule families now participate in refresh-before-write and final journal freshness checks. Deleted sources are removed from their applicable cached scope; other sibling scopes remain available without acquiring broader authority. Workspace project-rule links escaping the root are rejected before content is read. Existing InstructionsLoaded hooks are retained, including their differing error handling. Legacy standalone loader APIs remain compatibility interfaces; this manifest is the primary Agent path.

Focused instruction/authorization verification: **16 pass, 0 fail, 98 assertions** across three files. The broader four-file run had **60 pass, 2 fail**, 209 assertions; both failures were 5 s askBtw timeouts, while the new project-instruction tests passed. Typecheck passed. This is not a complete-suite result. Universal provenance for skills/memory, additional-root policy, and transactional filesystem-policy locking remain incomplete.

Project-authored documentation, comments, and other content must be in English. The conversation with Marcelo may remain in Portuguese. ### Cited claim states outside summaries — 2026-10-15

The existing session evidence store now supports append-only model claims through record_claim. States are hypothesis, supported, and refuted. Supported/refuted records require exact redacted excerpts from existing request, denial, or tool sources; summaries and other claims cannot serve as source records. A revision must reference the latest revision and preserve its proposition. New propositions use new records. Invalid citations, forks, invalid fields, and records above 8000 UTF-8 bytes are refused before mutation. Restore validates the same citation and chronological revision invariants.

Original sources and older revisions remain immutable and paginated. Reading an old claim includes its current revision ID, current status, and superseded flag. Context exposes up to eight current claim previews with explicit omission counts and recovery instructions; it labels claims as model-authored interpretations rather than facts, instructions, or grants. Complete current claim state is included in goal review and fingerprinted across the review request. Oversized complete claim sets refuse review instead of silently omitting claims. Original request/denial review stays separate from model claims.

The primary Agent refreshes its context after recording a claim. Workers use their own session ledger and receive their current claim state on subsequent requests; they cannot record into the parent's ledger through this tool. Mode/role/profile lists, native-tool registration, introspection, and prompt guidance include the new session-bookkeeping tool. No external execution capability is added. Recovery/claim results are not recursively registered as original tool evidence.

Initial regression phase: the three new ledger tests failed because recordClaim did not exist. Final focused verification: **178 pass, 0 fail, 504 assertions**, ten files, 7.29 s. This includes a real compact/persist/resume path with a simulated model returning an incorrect summary, immutable cited refutations, transitive corrections, Unicode/response bounds, secret redaction, worker isolation, and refusal of an approving goal reviewer when claim state changes during its request. Typecheck and diff-check passed. The earlier complete-suite result remains historical; no complete suite or model eval was repeated for this change.

Limits: exact citation matching establishes only that the quoted redacted text exists in a retained source, not that the model's interpretation is true, the source is current, or every assertion was recorded. This does not establish semantic compaction fidelity or measured LLM quality. Automatic extraction of all facts/criteria, dependency prioritization, and the remaining roadmap gaps still require work; the goal remains active.


### Source-bound goal criteria and per-item acceptance — 2026-10-15

Goal completion now has separate tool-free extraction and review stages. Extraction derives typed criteria from the original objective and retained request/denial sources, with exact source excerpts and test/inspection/external verification methods. Runtime-generated criterion IDs remain stable while the source fingerprint matches. The goal stores the manifest and the last version-bound assessment; get_goal exposes both, and context shows a bounded preview. New user corrections invalidate extraction. Existing goals without criteria extract them on their next completion attempt, not automatically before initial work.

Review must assess every criterion exactly once as satisfied, missing, or unverified, with nonempty reasons. A satisfied item needs exact citations to retained tool evidence; requests, summaries, model claims, bookkeeping, and delegated model conclusions cannot establish fulfillment. Known get_goal/todo/memory/worker-summary sources are rejected as fulfillment proof. The reviewer separately assesses whether extraction covers the original scope. An overall approval cannot bypass missing IDs, duplicates, unsupported citations, incomplete coverage, or unverified items. Required test criteria additionally need the latest verification receipt with passed status, ok=true, exit code zero, a positive integer test count, and matching current input/final workspace digests. Inspection/external items need a non-test observation; a passed suite alone cannot establish inspection or deployment.

The runtime snapshots the initial goal instead of holding a mutable reference. Before storing extraction and after review, it rechecks goal identity, current requests, and the manifest; final acceptance also rechecks workspace, claim state, verification, and evidence-index identity. Changed state refuses completion. Both model requests use the configured reviewer, account for returned usage, share cancellation/deadline handling, and expose no tools. Extraction/review records and the evidence index have explicit size limits; oversized or malformed records refuse completion instead of silently dropping requirements. Existing current-source limits still apply.

A storage regression exposed shared textual redaction consuming a JSON closing quote after an inline credential assignment inside a source snapshot. The real save/load test failed with an unterminated snapshot string. Assignment scanning now respects quote delimiters; valid object/array JSON strings are redacted structurally, including escaped quoted credentials, while unchanged ordinary JSON keeps its formatting. Reviewer JSON is parsed before structural redaction as well. Tests cover repeated redaction, absent canaries, intact source snapshots, and typed-criteria recovery through the actual session store.

Verification: the initial criterion test failed because the parser module did not exist. Focused parser/goal/reviewer tests passed after implementation, retaining workspace/source-change and oversized-source refusal cases. Final expanded run: **257 pass, 0 fail, 952 assertions**, fourteen files, 18.30 s, including goal, criteria, source/compaction, session/privacy, shell privacy, completion gates, verification runner, bot reviewer, bots, workers, and mode documentation. Typecheck and diff-check passed. No complete suite, real reviewer/model call, or model eval was executed; the canceled eval remains canceled.

Limits: exact excerpts and structured coverage declarations do not prove that extraction found every requirement, that referenced documents were fully inspected, or that cited checks cover each criterion. Inspection/external evidence is still semantically judged by the model; it has no universal independent live checker. A host without a current registered verification receipt cannot satisfy a test criterion; this change does not silently execute project commands or add execution permissions. Hierarchical review beyond the explicit limits and early extraction before work remain incomplete. These limits and the other roadmap gaps keep the overall goal active.


### Authorized verification receipts without a UI — 2026-10-15

The shell tool now accepts verification=true only for foreground execution with an owning runtime evidence ledger. The caller still supplies the command; after the workspace fingerprint, it must exactly match detectVerificationCommand for that project. Arbitrary commands cannot manufacture verification receipts. The existing shell policy, profile, script/version checks, cancellation, network restrictions, and Linux sandbox still apply. This path never calls the unsandboxed host verification executor. Unsupported sandbox environments refuse execution and record an inconclusive result.

runVerification reuses an injected captured-process executor for this path. Actual exit status, positive recognized test count, input/final workspace digests, timeout/cancellation, and capture/drain completeness determine the receipt. Failed, empty, changing, incomplete, timed-out, or canceled runs cannot pass. The full redacted receipt is stored as verification evidence; the returned JSON has a bounded 2000-character output preview, previewComplete, capture flags, and evidenceId for paginated recovery. Preview completeness describes the retained receipt, not the original process stream. Goal acceptance uses the same passed-receipt predicate and rejects inconsistent incomplete-capture flags.

The primary Agent installs its recorder regardless of whether a UI verification handler exists. Workers override that callback with their own ledger, preventing parent-recorder access. A current passing shell receipt from the same turn can satisfy the post-edit UI gate without a duplicate verification prompt; it is rechecked against current inputs first. Changed files or a new turn cannot reuse that shortcut. The existing UI host runner remains a separate legacy path; its cross-platform containment is not established by this change.

The initial three tests failed because shell returned ordinary text and did not implement the new mode. Real Linux sandbox regressions now cover credential isolation, failed/empty/stale suites, a 2.1-million-character capture overflow, bounded receipt previews, timeout/cancellation, refusal of arbitrary/background commands, no-UI Agent receipt/goal acceptance, normal permission denial, worker ledger isolation, and same-turn UI reuse versus mutation after testing. Expanded deterministic run: **181 pass, 0 fail, 573 assertions**, twelve files, 20.71 s. Typecheck and diff-check passed. No real model or model eval ran; the canceled eval remains canceled.

Limits: the project command detector still selects one known runner and does not prove that its selected suite covers the task or that a project checker is valid. This does not establish semantic criterion coverage, remote-effect validation, process reattachment, universal execution envelopes, or identical sandbox support on other operating systems. The remaining audit gaps keep the overall goal active.

### Cancellation at the final file-version boundary — 2026-10-15

### Side questions during a live foreground process — 2026-10-15

The C53 composition test now drives Agent.run through its normal tool gates with an injected local model response and a real sandboxed foreground Bun job. File barriers prove that the job started, remains responsive after the side request, and finishes only after release. Both successful side answers and explicitly cancelled side requests leave the main turn signal active; the job runs exactly once and its result reaches the next main response. A real passing suite in a local fixture clone cannot satisfy the other clone; a controlled branch change with unchanged files during execution produces stale rather than passed evidence. Existing Agent consent checks, resume reconciliation, same-turn receipt reuse and goal review consume the shared fingerprint. This closes the receipt-identity defect; it does not add a universal envelope to every tool result or establish executable/runtime provenance.

### Content-filter subprocesses in Git inspection and worktrees — 2026-10-15

Disabling fsmonitor, external diff and textconv did not prevent Git clean/smudge/process filters. Three controlled fixtures each executed a configured helper before the correction. Git filtering can also alter stored/materialized bytes; simply disabling a helper as an optional filter would silently substitute untransformed content. The implementation follows the [documented filter semantics](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitattributes#_filter): it enumerates only filter configuration keys, clears each known driver's executable commands for the child invocation, and requires that driver. A command that actually needs the unavailable transformation therefore fails instead of claiming equivalent output. No repository configuration is edited and filter command values are not returned to the model.

The shared runGitInspection boundary applies this guard to status/diff/log and worktree add/index/apply operations, retaining existing environment, helper, timeout and cancellation controls. Fingerprint/environment queries and post-edit review use the same boundary. Git-tool read operations use it; general Git-tool mutations remain separate. Config discovery is bounded to 64,000 bytes and aborts the operation on discovery errors. Drivers with mixed-case/dotted names are covered. Configured filters that do not apply to the files remain usable without running their helpers; dirty status can also succeed from metadata without requiring a transformation.

The corrected filter fixtures completed with **4 pass, 0 fail**, 19 assertions (327 ms). They cover clean, smudge, long-running process startup, required failure, preserved source contents and indexed blobs, Git-tool diff refusal, and an unrelated configured filter. Typecheck passed. These results do not establish atomicity against concurrent/conditional configuration changes, support for authorized filtered checkout creation, rollback of every preparatory Git effect, or OS containment of all Git subprocesses. A filter-dependent worktree may be refused; the manager's existing fallback remains serialized path-validated writing, never an uncontained writer shell. Native filter fixtures skip Windows. No real model eval was executed. Complete-suite repetition is recorded separately once terminal.

### Delegated Git authorization freshness and coordinator messages — 2026-10-15

### Stash restoration without repository-configured merge programs — 2026-10-15

Two local fixtures confirmed that Git stash pop could still execute a configured merge program after hooks and content filters were controlled. Both an attribute-selected driver and merge.default executed the marker helper before the correction. The helper returned success without resolving the changed file, demonstrating why replacing driver semantics with an automatic successful text merge would be unsafe.

Before automatic stash restoration, the shared Git boundary now enumerates only merge-driver configuration keys with the existing 64,000-byte capture cap and complete-capture checks. Each known driver command is replaced for that invocation with a fixed failing command. A merge that needs it therefore reports a conflict, retains the stash and keeps the current-branch file as the unresolved result instead of running repository code or claiming resolution. Repository configuration is not modified. The behavior follows the [documented custom-driver exit-status contract](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitattributes#_defining_a_custom_merge_driver), and the Git tool description discloses the conflict/retention behavior.

Fixtures verify both attribute/default selection with mixed-case dotted driver names, actual stash-ref retention, preserved current file contents and unmerged index entries. A nonconflicting restoration succeeds and drops the applied stash without executing the configured but unneeded helper. An oversized driver inventory is refused before restoration, preserving the stash and working-copy source. The focused Git-mutation, filter, inspection, identity and primary/worker authorization run completed with **47 pass, 0 fail**, 223 assertions across six files (9.66 s). ### Optional local package/import graph — 2026-10-16

The previously absent roadmap component now has a local TS/JS/package.json implementation in `src/agent/dependencyGraph.ts`, exposed through the existing read-only `lsp` tool with `operation: "dependency_graph"`. A file anchor selects imports/dependents; a package.json anchor selects package declarations. Direction and depth (0–3) control the neighborhood. No configured language server, project scripts, installed configuration plugins, watcher or first-use indexing step is required. The existing TypeScript dependency is loaded when queried and bundled for distribution; no dependency was added.

The TypeScript syntax parser extracts literal imports, exports, import-equals, require and dynamic imports, preserving source line and syntax kind. The resolution host sees only authorized catalog entries and virtual links for declared package.json workspaces. Aliases, local JSONC configuration inheritance, conditional import/require exports and manifest changes participate in resolution. Unsupported inheritance, unreadable configuration, unresolved imports, parse diagnostics and limits are reported rather than converted into semantic certainty. In particular, an oversized visible tsconfig does not trigger invented default resolution. Declared dependencies, resolved imports and package-membership edges retain distinct types, origins and source hashes; external declarations do not prove installation. Package nodes expose script names, never execute commands.

Every query rechecks the bounded file catalog and reads current content. Parsing and resolved-import results are cached within the session/context using content hashes; unchanged queries reuse both, source-only changes refresh that file, and catalog/configuration changes conservatively invalidate resolutions. Same-mtime source/ignore edits, new/deleted imports, changed aliases/exports/dependency declarations, changed protected scope, and mutable returned-edge isolation have dedicated fixtures. Final input identity/catalog checks reject observed concurrent changes. These checks do not provide an atomic snapshot against every external writer.

Limits include 2,000 catalog files, 20,000 directory entries, 256,000 bytes per input, 8,000,000 read bytes, a 2,000,000-byte parsing/resolution cache per root (at most four roots per owner), 10,000 indexed edges/resolutions, 100,000 resolution probes, and a 32,000-byte serialized response. Neighborhoods cap at 100 nodes and 200 edges with explicit omissions/incompleteness. Symlinks, sensitive paths, protected paths and ignored paths are excluded. Cancellation is checked during reads, traversal, periodic resolution and final validation.

This remains a partial roadmap implementation: installed dependency layout, pnpm/Nx/Bazel/Cargo/Maven/Gradle adapters, semantic symbol references/callers, test-coverage associations and general multi-language intelligence are absent. The bounded catalog/read cost is still paid per query, and configuration/catalog invalidation is conservative. The standalone bundle fixture proves parser availability without project node_modules; it does not establish cold/warm performance or model quality. Real model evals remain canceled, and these local checks cannot close the other research requirements.

The focused graph/tool/schema/interaction-mode run completed with **113 pass, 0 fail**, 568 assertions across four files (3.01 s). ### Session-owned language-server connections and document versions — 2026-10-16

The previous client started a server and opened version 1 for each query. `src/lsp.ts` now keeps configured servers within the owning orchestration session, separated by canonical workspace and server configuration. Queries on one connection are serialized and correlated by JSON-RPC IDs; separate sessions do not share processes or documents. Unowned direct calls remain short-lived. Changed settings, project-root changes, session shutdown, idle expiry, timeout, cancellation and protocol failure terminate the relevant connection. Agent shutdown signals its language servers before its first await, and orchestration shutdown independently closes owned servers. The protocol remains [stdio LSP](https://microsoft.github.io/language-server-protocol/specifications/lsp/3.17/specification/); server-originated workspace edits are refused.

Open documents retain content hashes and increasing versions; unchanged text does not increment the version. Each tool query rereads other open documents through current path/ignore/protected-scope checks, synchronizes edits and closes deleted documents. A changed document on a server that does not advertise change synchronization is refused and its connection discarded, allowing a subsequent fresh connection. Responses now wrap the server result with canonical workspace, server name, a unique connection-generation identity, hashed settings identity and the queried document's path/hash/version. Generation identity distinguishes version counters after restarts. The tool rechecks source contents and original path resolution before returning; observed changes discard the result and connection. This is source provenance, not a diagnostic/build/test receipt or proof of compiler correctness.

File reads reject oversized, non-file, invalid UTF-8 and NUL-containing inputs and check file identity while reading. Limits are 512 KiB per document, 2 MiB total open text, 32 open documents, four connections per owner, 32 queued requests per connection, 8 KiB headers and 1 MiB protocol frames/buffers. Retention evicts documents with didClose; old notifications are consumed without accumulating a history. Pending and queued cancellation have separate behavior: cancelling a queued caller does not interrupt another caller's active request. Shutdown/timeout also interrupts an asynchronous document refresh that has not finished. Server errors are reported without dumping arbitrary error payloads. Configured executables receive the existing scrubbed environment, excluding inherited provider credentials. POSIX termination targets the owned process group, including helpers.

The subprocess fixtures cover actual server reuse, version progression, other-open-file refresh, concurrent requests, settings/root/shutdown lifecycle, cancellation, timeout, malformed/oversized protocol responses, bounded retention, deleted files, unsupported synchronization, credential scrubbing, refused server edits, changing protected scope, source changes during a pending reply, bounded text reads and owned helper termination. The initial persistence fixture failed against the old client before implementation. These servers implement a deterministic local protocol fixture; no real model or third-party language server was used.

Remaining gaps: versioned diagnostics and rename proposals/application are still absent. Refreshing known open documents does not prove project-wide synchronization of unopened dependencies, generated files, build flags or configuration inheritance; server restarts/settings hashes are not a complete build-generation identity. Configured servers/plugins remain trusted executables with host filesystem/network access, despite environment scrubbing. POSIX process groups cannot contain helpers that deliberately escape their group, and Windows helper-tree termination is not established. Source rechecks are not an atomic transaction against every external writer. These limitations and the other audit gaps keep the research implementation goal active.

The focused LSP/navigation/orchestration/tool/mode run completed with **141 pass, 0 fail**, 584 assertions across seven files (4.77 s). The first complete isolated repetition, snapshot 8c9ec562b2cd8833a468303b8c07f8a4ad9bb36e, completed with **2660 pass, 4 skip, 2 fail**, 7847 assertions across 244 files (199.07 s); typecheck exited 0. One failure was the existing legacy-decision migration test exceeding its unchanged five-second timeout. Its entire file passed separately in the same copied checkout (**11 pass, 0 fail**, 61 assertions, 665 ms), without implementation or timeout changes; that repetition does not establish the original timeout's cause.

The other failure exposed a race in the new protocol fixture: it inspected the server log immediately after a hover response, before the server had consumed the client's refused-edit reply. The fixture now gates that hover response on receiving the client reply, so the assertion still requires the actual applied:false response. No delay or relaxed assertion was substituted. The corrected LSP file completed with **16 pass, 0 fail**, 44 assertions (2.76 s). A complete repetition with the corrected fixture is recorded separately after it reaches a terminal state. This replaces a thirty-millisecond ordering assumption that could accidentally cancel the first active request and leave fixture cleanup racing later tests. ### Version-bound document diagnostics — 2026-10-16

The read-only `lsp` tool now supports `operation: "diagnostics"` for a file without a cursor position. The client advertises publication-version support and document pull support. A server advertising diagnosticProvider receives a textDocument/diagnostic request; a full report is required because this client does not send a previous result ID. Pull results use versionBinding=request, distinguishing client-request provenance from an explicit server version. Otherwise, published diagnostics are accepted only for an open document and its exact version; older/future versions and unopened URIs are ignored. Unversioned publications return an explicit unversioned/unverified state without exposing their items as current-source diagnostics. Missing publications time out rather than becoming an empty clean report. These distinctions follow the protocol's [publication](https://github.com/microsoft/language-server-protocol/blob/gh-pages/_specifications/lsp/3.17/language/publishDiagnostics.md) and [pull-report](https://github.com/microsoft/language-server-protocol/blob/gh-pages/_specifications/lsp/3.17/language/pullDiagnostics.md) contracts.

Reports identify mode, version binding, observation time, cache reuse, omissions, requested-document scope and unverified build context. They retain only range, message, severity, code and source. Related-document reports and arbitrary server data are not forwarded. Shape/range validation refuses malformed reports, and each retained report caps at 200 items and 65,536 diagnostic-content bytes. The aggregate publication cache caps at 262,144 serialized bytes and is bounded by open-document ownership. Omissions are explicit; an empty versioned report differs from an absent report, and no diagnostic result contains a passed verification status.

Document versions now increase across the connection, including reopening an evicted/closed document, preventing delayed old reports from matching a reused version. Synchronizing changed/opened text clears cached publications; if a push-only query has no retained report, its open ownership is refreshed with a new version to request a fresh publication rather than reuse an unversioned old observation. Deleted/evicted documents discard their reports. Returned cache data is copied. Existing source/path rechecks also apply to diagnostics: a gated fixture changes the file while publication is pending and the tool refuses the response. Cancellation of a pending publication rejects without returning an empty report.

The first pushed-diagnostics regression timed out against the prior implementation, which discarded notifications. This includes 25 LSP subprocess cases covering stale/future/foreign publications, unversioned/empty/missing reports, bounded output, pull provenance, malformed reports, invalid unchanged pulls, source changes, cache isolation, reopen version identity and cancellation. The installed /usr/bin/rust-analyzer resolves to Rustup and its version probe reported that the component is not installed; no installation or real-server validation was performed. Real model evals remain canceled.

Limits: a server-confirmed document version is not proof of compiler correctness or dependency/build-generation freshness. Cached observations retain their original observation time. The current implementation does not provide workspace diagnostics, related-document reports, diagnostic code actions, complete generated/configuration-input identity or controlled rename. No real-server multi-language validation or model-quality/performance claim is established by these fixtures. The larger research objective remains active.

### Controlled LSP text-rename previews and range application — 2026-10-16

The read-only `lsp` tool now exposes rename_preview with an explicit file, UTF-16 cursor and new symbol name. A configured renameProvider is required; advertised prepareRename support is used before requesting edits. The client advertises UTF-16 positions and refuses a server selecting another encoding. It supports both plain changes and versioned documentChanges, preferring documentChanges when both are supplied according to the [workspace-edit contract](https://github.com/microsoft/language-server-protocol/blob/gh-pages/_specifications/lsp/3.17/types/workspaceEdit.md). Resource operations and change annotations are refused as a whole, rather than silently dropping unsupported effects or bypassing annotation confirmation.

The first rename request discovers affected text files without applying anything. Every new target passes the context-authorized bounded reader and is synchronized with the server; the request is then repeated so returned ranges refer to known client snapshots. Scope may expand for at most three rounds. Targets must remain unchanged, retained and within the authorized file scope; explicit returned versions must match. Unstable scope, stale versions, inaccessible targets, overlapping edits and exceeded budgets refuse the complete preview. No partial actionable edit list is returned. The result distinguishes proposal-only from no-edits and includes per-file canonical arguments, base hashes, document versions, proposed hashes and before/after snippets. Proposed hashes describe a potential result, not an observed write or verification receipt.

Application uses the existing edit_file writer with an alternative text_edits mode and required expected_hash. It does not introduce a write operation inside the read-only LSP tool. The normal executor's mode/profile/grant, project-rule, checkpoint and read-before-edit handling continues to apply to edit_file. Range application additionally requires a real execution context, checks the preview hash against both the current file and any runtime last-read version, and passes that base version into the existing leased atomic writer for its final comparison/cancellation check. A model-supplied hash cannot replace a conflicting runtime read version. Writes remain atomic per file; separately applying several files is not a workspace transaction. A stale proposal must be regenerated.

`src/tools/shared/textEdits.ts` shares bounded source reading between LSP navigation and range application. UTF-8 decoding now retains BOM bytes, so source hashes and edited snapshots agree with the actual file. Simultaneous ranges refer to one original document, preserve ordered insertions and reject overlap, reversed/out-of-document coordinates, surrogate-pair splits and invalid UTF-8 replacements. Bytes outside edited ranges, mixed endings and the final newline state are retained; uniformly CRLF documents normalize replacement newlines with an explicit result flag. Piece assembly avoids repeatedly rewriting the intermediate document. The existing line/substrings mode remains available.

Limits: 20 affected files, 200 total rename edits, at most three discovery rounds, 512 KiB per original/result document, 2 MiB synchronized target text and a 32,000-byte rename-result budget. The shared writer also bounds range-edit count and aggregate replacement bytes. Limits reject a whole preview rather than truncate actionable edits. The [rename](https://github.com/microsoft/language-server-protocol/blob/gh-pages/_specifications/lsp/3.17/language/rename.md) and [simultaneous edit-ordering](https://github.com/microsoft/language-server-protocol/blob/gh-pages/_specifications/lsp/3.17/types/textEditArray.md) contracts informed the protocol handling; local runtime tests remain the implementation evidence.

The initial range-edit fixture failed before the shared implementation existed. The focused LSP/range/legacy-edit/cancellation/mode/tool/schema/primary-authorization run completed with **175 pass, 0 fail**, 749 assertions across eight files (7.41 s); typecheck and diff-check passed. Subprocess fixtures verify two-query target discovery, both workspace-edit forms, actual per-file application, stale preview/read versions, untouched files during preview/refusal, unsupported URI/resource/annotation/encoding cases, no-edits results, edit/output quotas, unstable scope and changes while the synchronized reply is pending. Pure/file fixtures cover UTF-16, BOM, CRLF, insertion ordering, overlap, invalid Unicode, schema mode separation and aborted application.

Remaining gaps: file create/delete/rename resource operations, annotated edits requiring confirmation, code actions and universal semantic/dynamic-reference coverage are not implemented by this preview path. No workspace-wide transaction, complete build-generation identity or protection against every external-writer race is claimed. No real language server or model eval ran. Complete-suite results are recorded separately after reaching a terminal state. The research implementation goal remains active.

### Real TypeScript server/compiler integration and initialization options — 2026-10-16

`tests/lsp-real-server.test.ts` adds an opt-in integration case using an actual installed TypeScript Language Server and compiler. It creates its own two-file project, server HOME and source snapshots, then exercises definition, hover, diagnostic provenance, rename previews, ReadFile versions, hash-checked EditFile application and independent compiler invocations. A known TS2322 error is present before rename and remains afterward; fixing its annotation then produces compiler exit 0, failed=false and empty stdout/stderr. The subsequent navigation query reports the new source hash/version. Rename previews leave files unchanged until the writer is invoked, and both the declaration and imported uses are checked after application.

This run used Linux, Node.js 24.13.0, typescript-language-server 6.0.2 and TypeScript 6.0.3. Packages were installed with lifecycle scripts disabled into a private temporary prefix/cache, using explicit public-registry metadata and empty npm configuration files. Project dependencies/lockfiles and global SDK installations were not changed. The initially fetched TypeScript 7.0.2 package lacked lib/tsserver.js, which the adapter requires; the documented TypeScript 6 setup was installed in the temporary prefix instead. This is a pinned test combination, not a claim that every server/compiler release is compatible.

The first cross-file-definition assertion ran before semantic initialization and observed the adapter's local import binding. The installed server's syntax-routing implementation and protocol trace showed that cold navigation can use a syntax process while the project loads. The real fixture now uses an actual semantic diagnostic publication as its readiness milestone, retaining the cross-file definition assertion after that milestone. No arbitrary readiness sleep or relaxed target-URI assertion was substituted. A separate fresh-server rename probe with the default adapter configuration returned only the declaration file, demonstrating that source hashes alone cannot establish semantic completeness of a provider's result.

User-owned LSP server settings now accept bounded initializationOptions, which are forwarded in initialize. A deterministic protocol regression failed against the prior client, which dropped those options. Settings and direct runtime validation reject non-object or over-65,536-byte JSON payloads before starting a server. The relevant LSP configuration is cloned before asynchronous work so concurrent caller mutation cannot change the options associated with an already computed configuration identity; changes invalidate owned connections. Existing settings scope sanitization continues to remove the entire LSP configuration from project/local authority. The real fixture also starts a fresh server configured with tsserver.useSyntaxServer=never and requires its first rename request to include both files. This follows the adapter's [initialization configuration](https://github.com/typescript-language-server/typescript-language-server/blob/HEAD/docs/configuration.md), rather than a generic claim that all providers expose semantic readiness.

The real server publishes diagnostics without a document version. Version-bound items remain empty and status/versionBinding remain unversioned/unverified. The client now exposes bounded, shape-validated messages separately under unverified.items as investigation hints. Top-level omission metadata explains their exclusion from version-bound evidence; the separate hint object reports its own omissions/truncation. Those messages do not become compiler/test receipts. Mock regressions preserve the earlier empty version-bound-items assertions and add hint/large-publication quota checks; the independent compiler confirms the real fixture's diagnostic instead of treating an unversioned publication as proof.

### Bounded edit-only LSP code actions — 2026-10-16

The read-only LSP tool now exposes code_actions for a 1-based UTF-16 range and code_action_preview for a native action_id from the same session/file. Optional kind filters include subkinds. Literal code action, disabled/preferred, opaque data and edit-resolution capabilities are advertised; a provider must advertise support. Cached push/pull diagnostics overlapping the range supply context with explicit version-binding metadata. Unversioned diagnostics remain investigation hints, not validation receipts.

Listings retain at most 32 actions, 64 KiB per raw action, 256 KiB of raw action payload and 16,000 bytes of descriptors. Omitted actions are counted. Only IDs, titles, kinds, preference and availability status leave the native cache; provider data and command arguments are not accepted from the model or echoed in the tool result. Another listing, observed document changes/evictions, configuration changes and server disposal invalidate IDs. A listing nonce prevents identical later listings from reauthorizing an earlier ID.

Preview re-queries the original range/context and requires exactly one matching action, comparing all original metadata except the edit being recomputed. Advertised deferred edit resolution preserves that metadata. Changed, ambiguous, disabled or command-dependent actions fail the whole preview. In particular an edit-plus-command action is not silently reduced to its edit, and workspace/executeCommand is never sent. Rename and code actions share scope-authorized target discovery, synchronized versions, bounded rounds, complete text-edit validation and a 32,000-byte result budget including selection metadata. Application continues through existing ReadFile/EditFile permissions, source hashes, runtime read versions and atomic per-file writes.

The dedicated subprocess fixtures cover direct/deferred/multi-file edits; command-only, edit-plus-command and resolved-command refusal; disabled/ambiguous/changed actions; stale versions; unsafe/resource/annotated targets; source/configuration lifetime; diagnostic context; malformed providers/ranges; retention quotas; and source changes while a query is pending. The initial pending-query fixture asserted a promise before releasing its server handshake, causing a timeout; moving the assertion after the explicit release fixed the fixture without changing production timeout or acceptance rules. The dedicated current run completed with **29 pass, 0 fail**, 97 assertions (1.61 s).

The opt-in real TypeScript fixture additionally requests source.removeUnusedImports, obtains the actual adapter's edit-only action, verifies preview does not write, applies its arguments through ReadFile/EditFile and checks the exact resulting file plus independent compiler exit 0 with empty output. Additional retention/lifecycle regressions and conservative metadata validation followed that run; the complete isolated current-source repetition is recorded separately once terminal.

Providers with changing opaque identity metadata may require relisting; matching by title alone is intentionally insufficient. No model eval ran and no model-quality improvement is claimed. ### Owned process identity and restart liveness reconciliation — 2026-10-16

Background Shell runners now register the actual native ChildProcess with their task attempt. The registry stores a typed optional process observation in its existing versioned snapshot, validates that structure on restore and emits process_observed events for native registration/exit. Old snapshots remain readable without inventing identities from historical PID strings. On Linux, identity includes boot UUID, PID namespace, PID and kernel start-time ticks; stat parsing handles names containing spaces/parentheses/newlines, and bounded reads avoid unbounded process metadata. The [kernel proc interface](https://www.kernel.org/doc/html/latest/filesystems/proc.html) supplies these process fields; no process command line or environment is collected.

Registry status/list queries re-observe the recorded identity. They distinguish alive, exited, identity-mismatch and unknown with timestamps/provenance. Missing identity, unavailable kernel information and unsupported platforms remain unknown; a different boot/namespace/start time cannot authorize reuse of a PID. A missing/zombie leader is not a completed task or a reconstructed exit status. Only an exit event observed by the owning native executor supplies its actual code/signal. Every observation is explicitly scoped to the leader with progress unmeasured. These fields reach the model's runtime/resume context and the /tasks and /task status surfaces.

Restoring an unfinished attempt no longer asserts that its process stopped. The attempt remains interrupted with process/effect reconciliation required, even if its leader remains alive. Restored queued/blocked records carrying an earlier process are not re-enqueued. Tracked process failures are non-retryable automatically, including when the runner initially labels an error retryable; /task resume refuses replay and directs the caller to reconcile effects and submit a newly authorized command. Fresh executions continue through the existing shell permission/sandbox path. This does not discover, signal, restart or grant authority over an unrelated PID.

Local regressions exercise kernel identity mismatch, absent leaders without invented exit codes, native exit provenance, persisted real-process observations, no automatic retries and refusal of queued snapshot replay. An actual separate controller process starts a bounded detached worker, persists its native task identity and exits; a different process restores the snapshot, observes that worker alive, refuses replay, releases it through its fixture protocol and observes leader exit while keeping the task result unknown. Exactly one worker start is recorded. The first controller fixture embedded an unescaped newline in its generated worker source; String.raw fixed the fixture without changing production observation or replay gates. The focused repetition completed with **51 pass, 0 fail**, 216 assertions across five files (2.20 s); current-source typecheck exited 0. A cleanup-only follow-up waits for the fixture worker's observed exit before deleting its release path. Complete isolated results follow once terminal.

Remaining gaps: the journal does not reassociate anonymous stdout/stderr pipes after supervisor death or recover a lost tail/completion receipt; only complete records already present remain readable, with incomplete/`gapUnknown` reported. It does not supervise every descendant, prove useful progress, provide universal detached-job survival or validate remote jobs. Linux leader identity is the exercised platform; unavailable/unsupported identity stays unknown. A fresh authorized execution after manual effect reconciliation is distinct from automatic continuation. No model eval ran, and the larger research implementation goal remains active.

The real TypeScript LSP fixture remained enabled. The actual-controller-exit recovery fixture also ran; its process helper is private, bounded and cooperatively released. Model evals remain canceled. The overall research implementation goal remains active.

### Typed session grant history and denial continuity — 2026-10-16

`SessionEvidence` now stores tool and external-directory session approvals as typed grant events with a stable grant ID, scope, subject, timestamp and `active`/`revoked`/`expired` status. Creation, transition and persisted-event validation reject invalid identities, scope changes, duplicate activation and illegal lifecycle transitions. Explicit approval clearing, directory removal, workspace changes and agent changes close matching events. Restoring a transcript expires every remaining active grant before runtime use; the in-memory approval sets remain the only authorization source. The evidence context and recovery tool label grant records as audit data, never permission.

The authorization integration exercises a session approval followed by reuse, explicit revocation followed by a new prompt, restore followed by expiration, and external-directory removal. A separate compact/resume scenario records a user denial, runs the real compaction and session restoration paths with a simulated model client, verifies the denial remains a historical constraint with no live grant, then confirms a later attempt prompts again before execution. Existing denial handling already records user refusals; no denied action is converted into a grant.

The initial full-suite run exposed a no-op context refresh when switching agents with no active grants. `endSessionGrants` now reports whether it closed any event, so the prompt context refreshes only when the ledger changes. One-shot user decisions and PermissionRequest hook allow/block results are recorded as separate hash-bound authorization events; successful `always` selections record a persistent-policy event only after the policy write succeeds. Each tool-bound decision now carries the model's `toolCallId`, which matches the corresponding `tool` or `denial` evidence source; workflow tool consent passes the same ID, and the legacy risk-confirmation callback is covered for allow and deny. Persisted authorization events without an ID remain accepted for backward compatibility. The complete local deterministic run `bun test tests` after these changes completed with **2764 pass, 5 skip, 0 fail**, 8332 assertions across 248 files (195.10 s). Skips: opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, and Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed; no model call or eval ran. These events remain audit data rather than the execution gate. At this entry's cutoff, settings changes outside these prompt paths were uncovered; standalone `startWorkflow` consent had no model tool-call ID; `session_evidence` and `record_claim` omitted their recursive tool-result record; and the ledger could not establish whether consent was informed or whether external policy stayed unchanged outside existing runtime checks.

Correction: the recovery/claim tools' omission from recursive tool-result evidence is intentional, not an outstanding ledger gap.
