A regression assertion now keeps this boundary explicit.

### Repeated task failure circuit breaker — 2026-10-16

The task scheduler now retries generic failures only when they carry a recognized transient signal (HTTP 408/425/429/5xx, known network codes, transient provider class names, or AWS `$retryable` metadata) or an explicit retryable `TaskRuntimeError`; unclassified failures default to non-retryable. It stops automatic retries after three consecutive identical retryable failures when the runner reports no changed partial state. A different partial state resets the failure streak, and explicit resume, blocking, or success clears it. The primary agent now blocks an identical fourth failed tool call before execution after three failures; a successful tool call clears the current streaks. The focused run across `tests/task-registry.test.ts`, `tests/process-reconciliation.test.ts` and `tests/completionGates.test.ts` completed with **48 pass, 0 fail**, 198 assertions (12.82 s); typecheck and diff-check passed. Other provider/progress semantics remain unclassified or not semantically validated, and no causal model comparison was performed.

### Recoverable shell output on storage failure — 2026-10-16

Shell output artifacts now fall back to a bounded process-local memory cache when disk creation or writing fails. The same secret-redacted, UTF-16-bounded content and channel provenance are retained. Disk retention remains capped at 10 artifacts/20 MiB per session; the memory fallback is capped globally at 10 artifacts/20 MiB, evicts the oldest entries, and is explicitly labeled as unavailable after process exit. A cleanup failure after a successful disk write returns the persisted artifact ID with a retention warning instead of discarding the recovery reference. If disk storage and the memory quota both fail, artifact creation still fails.

The focused run across `tests/shell-output-privacy.test.ts`, `tests/shell-stream-capture.test.ts` and `tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts` completed with **27 pass, 0 fail**, 138 expectations across three files (8.87 s). It covers redaction, bounded memory fallback and eviction, session isolation, provenance, background-task reattachment and stream capture. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model eval ran, and the overall research implementation goal remains active because other requirements remain partial or unverified.

### Large-file read-version consistency — 2026-10-16

Large byte-range reads now bind the full-content hash to a stable file identity across hashing and range capture. Identity checks include device, inode, mode, size, high-resolution modification time and change time before/after hashing, on the opened range-read handle, and on the path after capture. This prevents returning bytes from a changed same-size file with the earlier hash, including when its modification time is restored. A deterministic regression changes the file between hashing and range capture, restores the original mtime, and requires the read to fail without recording a file version.

The current focused run across `tests/research-readfile.test.ts`, `tests/shell-output-privacy.test.ts`, `tests/shell-stream-capture.test.ts` and `tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts` completed with **33 pass, 0 fail**, 187 expectations across four files (9.33 s). `bun run typecheck` exited 0. Dependency/toolchain/environment fingerprinting, the exact test read set, and the external-writer race at the final commit boundary remain unimplemented. No model eval ran, and the overall research implementation goal remains active.

### In-turn lifecycle hook process-tree cancellation — 2026-10-16

`Agent.abort()` now propagates its signal into PreToolUse and PermissionRequest command hooks, UserPromptSubmit/UserPromptExpansion, and in-turn compaction, Stop, and post-tool lifecycle hooks. PermissionRequest signals also reach subagent permission hooks. POSIX hook commands run in their own process group. Timeout or cancellation sends SIGTERM and escalates to SIGKILL; the close path also kills remaining group members so descendants that ignore SIGTERM or close inherited pipes cannot continue delayed side effects. Shell stream capture now shares the same process-group signaling helper.

The direct hook regression verifies cancellation prevents a delayed child-file write. Agent integration regressions call `Agent.abort()` while PreToolUse, PermissionRequest, and UserPromptSubmit hooks are running; they verify that neither the delayed child nor the tool/model loop runs. The focused run across `tests/agent-authorization.test.ts`, `tests/agent.test.ts`, `tests/streaming.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/claudeLifecycleHooks.test.ts`, `tests/hooks/codexLifecycleHooks.test.ts`, `tests/kernel/hooks.test.ts`, `tests/subagent-executor.test.ts` and `tests/shell-stream-capture.test.ts` completed with **147 pass, 3 skip, 0 fail**, 440 assertions across eight files (33.70 s). The skips are the three OAuth streaming cases. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.

The complete local deterministic `bun test` run after these hook changes completed with **2749 pass, 5 skip, 0 fail**, 8272 assertions across 248 files (237.56 s). The skips were the real TypeScript LSP server integration, three OAuth streaming cases, and the Windows-only browser launcher case. No model eval script or model calls ran. `bun run typecheck` passed after the source changes, as did final whitespace checks and the documentation language scan.

Limits: this does not establish cancellation for every lifecycle hook, background worker, or external tool. Windows process-tree termination and descendants that deliberately escape the POSIX process group remain unverified; a process outside that group cannot be contained by this mechanism. No model eval ran, and the overall research implementation goal remains active.

### Workflow replay excludes its own state directory — 2026-10-16

`WorkflowManager` now excludes its configured state directory from the workspace source fingerprint. The requested and resolved in-workspace paths are bound into the fingerprint, a state path that resolves outside the workspace fails closed, and excluding the workspace root is rejected. This keeps run/journal files created by a workflow from invalidating its own replay while source changes still invalidate it. Missing project roots are not created implicitly; callers must provide a real workspace.

The regression verifies state-file changes leave the source fingerprint stable, source edits change it, and the workspace root cannot be excluded. Workflow tool coverage resumes a saved run with an in-workspace state directory; lifecycle coverage refuses to replay a historical run without a context fingerprint. The first current-source full-suite run exposed seven fixtures that used nonexistent project roots and one outdated legacy-replay expectation. The fixtures now create their declared roots, and the old record is expected to fail closed. The focused run across `tests/workspace-version.test.ts`, `tests/workflows/tool.test.ts`, `tests/workflows/lifecycle.test.ts` and `tests/commands/batch.test.ts` completed with **35 pass, 0 fail**, 102 assertions across four files (1.43 s). The current complete deterministic run completed with **2763 pass, 5 skip, 0 fail**, 8321 assertions across 248 files (169.93 s). The skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, and the Windows-only browser launcher. No model eval or model call ran.

Typecheck and diff-check passed. The suite does not verify Windows/macOS behavior, and workflow replay still excludes installed dependency and toolchain state from the workspace fingerprint.

### Shell execution refuses missing runtime context or OS sandbox — 2026-10-16

Removed the direct unsandboxed Shell fallback. Shell commands now require an owning `ToolExecutionContext`; the existing Linux bubblewrap executor is the only command runner, and a contextual request returns an error before spawn when that sandbox is unavailable. This also removes the legacy global confirmation callback and its pipe/eval-runner setup; those paths already use their owning agent context and permission gates. Output recovery remains session-scoped.

A regression executes a write command through `Shell.execute` without context and confirms the marker file is never created. A second conditional regression checks the unsupported-sandbox path and is enabled on hosts without bubblewrap. The focused Shell/verification/path-boundary run completed with **37 pass, 1 skip, 0 fail**, 201 assertions across four files (13.38 s). The skip is the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux machine. Typecheck passed.

The current complete local deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2765 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8334 assertions across 248 files (181.85 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran. Contextual shell execution remains unavailable on macOS/Windows until a supported OS sandbox is implemented; those platforms and their process-tree behavior were not exercised here. The overall research implementation goal remains active.

### Verification receipts bind execution environment — 2026-10-16

Verification receipts now include an execution fingerprint in addition to the workspace digest. It hashes the selected runner and application runtime binaries, the effective allowlisted environment (host or the fixed bubblewrap environment), the bubblewrap launcher when used, and known npm/pnpm/Yarn installation-state files. Fingerprint values are hashes; raw environment values are not stored. If workspace or execution inputs change during a run, the result is stale. Same-turn receipt reuse and goal completion recompute the current execution fingerprint, so an old receipt cannot satisfy a test criterion after a detected runtime/environment/install-state change.

Regressions cover a changed npm installation-state file with unchanged workspace digest, effective environment changes, runner identity, stale goal-review fingerprints, and sandboxed receipts. The focused run across `tests/verify.test.ts`, `tests/shell-verification.test.ts` and `tests/goal-acceptance.test.ts` completed with **34 pass, 0 fail**, 193 assertions across three files. `bun run typecheck` passed.

The current complete deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2767 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8349 assertions across 248 files (180.55 s). Skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. Typecheck and `git diff --check` passed. No model calls or evals ran.

Limits: the bounded fingerprint omits dependency locations outside the known local roots and does not discover every compiler/process launched inside arbitrary test scripts; exact test read sets and atomic protection from external writers remain open. It supplements the workspace digest but does not establish semantic test coverage. No LLM eval or model call ran, and the overall research implementation goal remains active.

### Verification receipts hash bounded local dependency trees — 2026-10-16

Execution fingerprints now include content hashes for known local dependency roots: `node_modules`, `.yarn/cache`, `vendor`, and the selected project `.venv`/`venv` when verification invokes that interpreter. Directory structure, modes, symlink targets and dependency bytes participate in the digest. Symlinks escaping the project and unsupported entries fail closed. Per-file hashing uses bounded concurrency; directory, file, symlink, and initially missing-root identities are rechecked after the content pass to reject a tree that changes during capture. Traversal has explicit 100,000-entry and 4-GiB limits. Small-file reads use a bounded in-memory path; larger files use streaming hashes.

The final focused run across `tests/verify.test.ts`, `tests/shell-verification.test.ts` and `tests/goal-acceptance.test.ts` completed with **36 pass, 0 fail**, 198 assertions across three files (9.65 s). The complete local deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2769 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8354 assertions across 248 files (207.25 s); the six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. A fresh-process fingerprint of the roughly 223-MiB dependency tree took 3.1 s with the filesystem cache warm; the first observed run before final revalidation took 6.1 s, with filesystem-cache state uncontrolled. No model call or eval ran.

Remaining gaps: the 4-GiB/100,000-entry caps can make verification inconclusive; external package-store layouts are not fingerprinted; arbitrary child compilers/tools and exact test read sets remain unknown; and the receipt is not an atomic lock against writers after capture. This increases coverage for known local dependencies but does not establish semantic test coverage. The larger research implementation goal remains active.

### Interrupted attempts without process identities refuse replay — 2026-10-16

Task resume now requires reconciliation whenever a restored attempt carries the `INTERRUPTED` error, even when the original runner never registered a native process. Reattaching a runner alone cannot erase that interruption barrier. The task remains failed with its prior partials and artifacts; the caller must inspect effects and submit a newly authorized task rather than replaying a possibly side-effecting attempt.

The focused run across `tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts`, `tests/process-reconciliation.test.ts` and `tests/task-registry.test.ts` completed with **37 pass, 0 fail**, 161 assertions across three files (2.63 s). The complete local deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2769 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8355 assertions across 248 files (210.93 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` passed. No model call or eval ran.

This closes the no-registered-process replay gap. Complete effect reconciliation, external process discovery, and reattachment/supervision of a live process group remain incomplete.

### ReadFile request and range validation — 2026-10-16

`read_file` now bounds path input before filesystem access: at most 32 paths, 4096 UTF-8 bytes per path, and 65536 serialized bytes for the path selector. It rejects simultaneous `path` and `paths`, requires safe-integer ranges, and rejects reversed line intervals. The JSON schema exposes matching per-field limits and disallows undeclared properties.

The new regressions first failed on an oversized path with `ENAMETOOLONG` and on a reversed line range that reached filesystem lookup. After the fix, `bun test tests/research-readfile.test.ts` completed with **9 pass, 0 fail**, 57 assertions, including runtime and schema-bound checks. A broader check across ReadFile, schema freshness, authorization and tool discovery completed with **47 pass, 0 fail**, 243 assertions across four files. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.

The path request bounds do not limit the I/O required to hash an entire large file for stable byte-range reads; returned bytes remain capped separately. No provider tokenizer was added, so these caps do not equal token limits.

### Background Shell workspace-change observations — 2026-10-16

Background Shell execution now captures a source-input workspace fingerprint and persists it before starting the command. After exit, cancellation, failure or timeout, it records whether the observed workspace fingerprint changed. Restoring an interrupted task with a pending observation safely resolves its project-relative workspace path, recaptures the fingerprint and updates the failed result. A missing, moved or unobservable workspace stays `unknown`. Task resume remains blocked after this observation; the status does not authorize replay.

The result explicitly keeps `effects: not-reconciled`. A completed sandboxed command marks `processGroupSettled: settled` only after the bwrap leader exits and output pipes drain; an interrupted snapshot remains `unknown`. A real sandbox regression starts a background descendant with redirected output and verifies it cannot write after the task reports completion. The fingerprint excludes ignored, generated, dependency and runtime-state paths, and it cannot establish network, database or other external effects. A no-change observation after restart is time-bounded and does not prove that a surviving descendant will not write later. Reconnecting output or supervising the entire process group across controller restart is still unsupported.

Regressions cover unchanged and modified workspaces after a live background command, changes between snapshot and restore, cancellation after a workspace write, and sandbox descendant termination. The focused run across `tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts`, `tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts`, `tests/task-registry.test.ts` and `tests/shell-stream-capture.test.ts` completed with **57 pass, 1 skip, 0 fail**, 229 assertions (10.88 s); the skip is the unsupported-sandbox condition on this host. The complete deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2771 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8362 assertions across 248 files (212.10 s), before the process-group-settlement follow-up. The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, the conditional unsupported-sandbox case, and the Windows-only browser launcher. Typecheck and diff-check passed on the final source. No model call or eval ran. Other partial and unverified requirements keep the overall research implementation goal active.

### Authorization settings revoke session approvals — 2026-10-16

`Agent.applySettings` now hashes a canonical form of effective permission rules, risk configuration, and authorization hooks. When that surface changes, it clears in-memory session approvals and records matching grants as revoked with reason `settings-changed`; reapplying an equivalent configuration preserves approvals. Historical one-shot decisions remain audit records and do not authorize future calls. The regression failed before the fix, then the focused authorization, session-evidence, and compaction run completed with **47 pass, 0 fail**, 264 assertions across three files (12.98 s). Typecheck passed. No model call or eval ran.

Original limit: the API-only invalidation did not observe out-of-band edits. The follow-up below adds policy reload checks at authorization and final execution boundaries.

### Transactional MCP catalog reload — 2026-10-16

MCP reloads now build and validate a replacement catalog before swapping it into the agent. A failed replacement leaves current tools and clients available. The previous client generation closes only after its in-flight tool calls finish. A reload that completes after a workspace switch or shutdown closes its newly loaded clients without installing them; shutdown waits for pending reload completion and retires all client generations.

Deterministic regressions cover load failure, slow replacement, an in-flight old-server call, approval-triggered reload sequencing, and shutdown during a pending load. `bun test tests/mcp.test.ts` completed with **51 pass, 0 fail**, 106 assertions. The current complete deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2785 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8425 assertions across 249 files (177.95 s); `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported-sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. No model call or eval ran.

### Generic delegated-worker aggregate budgets — 2026-10-16

Generic subagent tasks now inherit a per-turn budget-group ID. The task registry serializes model requests within each group, carries token and estimated-cost usage across sibling workers, and caps the next request's output tokens against the remaining allowance. Missing usage fails closed for further model calls; a response that exceeds the aggregate allowance does not execute its tool calls. Optional `agents.maxAggregateTokens` and `agents.maxAggregateCostUsd` settings configure these group-wide caps. Legacy snapshots without budget-group IDs infer consistent groups from their task ancestry.

Regressions cover shared sibling budgets, cancellation while queued for a request, parent-child inheritance, legacy snapshot ordering, output-token/cost caps, missing-usage behavior, and settings validation. The focused run across `tests/task-aggregate-budget.test.ts`, `tests/task-registry.test.ts` and `tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts` completed with **40 pass, 0 fail**, 145 assertions. The complete deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2781 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8408 assertions across 249 files (189.07 s). The skips were the opt-in real TypeScript LSP server, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported-sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck`, `git diff --check`, and the documentation accent scan passed. No model call or eval ran.

Limits: a single response can exceed the total-token cap through prompt usage or a provider that ignores the output limit. Prompt cost can exceed the remaining cost allowance before the response can be measured; cost is estimated from reported usage. Focused worker context and E11/E12 causal experiments remain open; model evals were not run.

### Fork workers receive declared dependency context — 2026-10-16

Fork-context workers now receive prior results only for task IDs they declared as dependencies. The bounded JSON includes each dependency's task, summary, confidence, files read/changed and issues found; it omits suggestions and arbitrary metadata. If no dependency is specified, existing fork behavior still includes all prior results. The caller wraps this JSON as untrusted data, so it cannot grant instructions or permissions.

`bun test tests/orchestration-fork-context.test.ts` completed with **2 pass, 0 fail**, 5 assertions. The regressions verify dependency filtering, empty matches, concrete file/issue references, bounded retained references, and the no-dependency fallback. A focused run including agent configuration, worker execution and task-registry tests completed with **60 pass, 0 fail**, 190 assertions across four files. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.

This is a bounded context-selection step, not evidence retrieval from arbitrary session history. It does not automatically decide which files or claims are necessary, and no model experiment measured the effect on worker quality or token use. E11/E12 remain unexecuted.

### Detached process-group observations survive controller restart — 2026-10-16

Linux process identities now retain the process-group ID when a tracked detached child owns its group. Restored snapshots reject a `process-group` scope unless its leader identity and group observation agree, and reject a group observation attached to `leader-only` scope. If the leader exits, the registry revalidates remaining members of that same group using bounded `/proc` observations and persists periodic heartbeat updates after task restoration. A task cannot report `done` while an owned group member remains or group state is unknown.

The regression runs a separate controller process that records a detached group leader, then exits while the leader also exits and leaves a child alive. A fresh registry restores the snapshot, sees the leader as exited and the group as alive, refuses replay, persists a later observation, then sees the group exit after the fixture releases its child. It also rejects snapshots with missing/mismatched group IDs or contradictory scopes. `bun test tests/process-reconciliation.test.ts` completed with **8 pass, 0 fail**, 68 assertions. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.

The complete deterministic run `bun test tests` completed with **2795 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8465 assertions across 250 files (192.06 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript LSP fixture, three OAuth streaming cases, the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran.

This section records Linux liveness supervision for a registered process that owns its group. The later Shell journal supports cursor-paged redacted output for eligible detached tasks after controller restart, but does not rebind anonymous pipes if the supervisor dies; only complete journal records remain readable and the result is incomplete/`gapUnknown`. Unregistered processes, descendants that leave the group, external effects, and non-Linux behavior remain outside this coverage. The broader research implementation goal remains active.

### Shell preview retains a diagnostic outside positional samples — 2026-10-16

The streaming Shell preview still samples the beginning, middle and end, and now retains one bounded line neighborhood when it detects common error, failure, exception, traceback, TypeScript diagnostic, or package-manager error markers. It labels this excerpt as heuristic, keeps ANSI stripping and per-channel progress handling, and does not replace full output recovery.

A regression puts an `ERROR_DIAGNOSTIC` marker outside all three positional samples in a 1.4-million-character stream and confirms the marker remains in the bounded preview while captured content stays exact. The focused run across Shell capture, harness feedback, and background Shell completed with **33 pass, 1 skip, 0 fail**, 150 assertions; the skip is the unsupported-sandbox test on this Linux host. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.

The classifier is intentionally heuristic and can miss compiler formats or select unrelated text. The Shell artifact remains the authoritative source for full log inspection. No model eval or quality claim was made; the broader research implementation goal remains active.

### Retrieved report text cannot override an explicit write denial — 2026-10-16

A deterministic authorization regression reads a report containing a fake system override, then passes its contents to `write_file`. An explicit `write_file(*)` deny remains authoritative, the call returns the blocked-by-rule result, and no destination file is created. The test also checks the system prompt classifies file and tool output as data, not instructions.

`bun test tests/agent-authorization.test.ts` completed with **27 pass, 0 fail**, 131 assertions. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model eval ran.

This proves the runtime permission rule cannot be granted by retrieved content. It does not establish model behavior on untrusted instructions, cover every output source or route, or replace the cancelled model eval. C58 and the broader research implementation goal remain partial and active.

### Permission rules match documented tool aliases — 2026-10-16

The shared permission matcher now normalizes documented CamelCase native tool names to their snake_case executor names, while retaining existing lowercase and custom tool-name matching. The settings UI and README use names such as `ReadFile` and `WriteFile`; before this change, `WriteFile(*)` silently failed to match the executor name `write_file`, so an apparent explicit deny could allow the call.

The regression was first run red against `WriteFile(*)`, then passed for both allow and deny rules, including the irregular public alias `MoA`. The C58 runtime test now uses the documented `WriteFile(*)` syntax and verifies that a report containing a fake system override cannot bypass the explicit deny. The focused run across permission matching, agent authorization, subagent permissions/execution, and permission reporting completed with **72 pass, 0 fail**, 268 assertions. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed.

A follow-up regression verifies the same documented alias in the delegated-worker executor. `bun test tests/subagent-executor.test.ts` completed with **29 pass, 0 fail**, 90 assertions.

This fixes tool-name alias matching in the shared permission-rule path. It does not close the broader route/platform enforcement audit or establish model behavior without the cancelled evals; the overall research implementation goal remains active.

The full deterministic run before the ReadFile fingerprint-limit follow-up, `bun test tests`, completed with **2798 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8476 assertions across 250 files (199.41 s). The skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported-sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran.

After that full run, a bounded whole-file fingerprint limit was added to ReadFile. It refuses files over 256 MiB before scanning, rechecks size at the hash boundary, and stops a streaming hash when the byte budget is crossed. The focused run across ReadFile, EditFile, PatchFile, and file-commit cancellation completed with **43 pass, 0 fail**, 133 assertions; `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. The focused subagent test above also ran after the full-suite snapshot. These final additions have focused coverage; they are not included in the 2798-test full-suite count.

### Serialized tool-result envelopes stay within the batch cap — 2026-10-16

The shared tool-output budget now measures each consecutive tool-result batch as serialized JSON, including result metadata, UTF-8 content, array delimiters, and escaping. If the empty result envelopes alone exceed 40,000 bytes, the batch is rejected rather than allowing metadata to bypass the cap. Oversized content still preserves recoverable evidence references when the envelope leaves enough room. The same shared path is used for primary-agent and delegated-worker requests.

The focused run across `tests/tool-output-budget.test.ts` and `tests/subagent-executor.test.ts` completed with **36 pass, 0 fail**, 141 assertions. No model call or eval ran.

The cap applies to each consecutive tool-result batch only. It does not cap assistant tool-call arguments or the full conversation, and serialized bytes do not equal provider tokens. The broader research implementation goal remains active.

### Delegated usage survives retries and budget uncertainty fails closed — 2026-10-16

Task metrics now add each delegated model response instead of replacing totals with the final attempt. Token- and cost-limited workers pass the remaining allowance as the request output ceiling where supported; if the provider omits usage needed to enforce a configured limit, the task fails closed. Cost-limited tasks also stop when returned usage cannot be priced. Parent session cost is labeled partial if any worker response lacks priceable usage.

The focused run across `tests/subagent-executor.test.ts`, `tests/task-aggregate-budget.test.ts`, `tests/task-registry.test.ts`, and `tests/orchestration-e2e.test.ts` completed with **66 pass, 0 fail**, 233 assertions. It covers retry accumulation, missing/unpriceable usage, request output ceilings, and partial-cost reporting. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran.

Provider-specific pricing and billing for responses lost before usage is returned remain unverified, as does reconciliation outside delegated workers. This closes only those local accounting and budget invariants; it makes no performance or model-quality claim.

After this change, `bun test tests` completed with **2807 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8497 assertions across 250 files (191.99 s). The skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` passed after the implementation; `git diff --check` passed after this audit update. No model call or eval ran.

### Provider-bound cost estimates and limits — 2026-10-16

Cost estimates now require a catalogued DeepSeek model and the official `api.deepseek.com` HTTPS endpoint. Vertex, Bedrock, local/custom gateways, unknown models, and incomplete prompt/completion usage are not assigned DeepSeek list prices. Session summaries and structured stats expose cost availability; verified prior amounts remain labeled as a partial subtotal when other usage cannot be priced. Per-task and aggregate dollar budgets fail before the model request when the provider/model rate is unverified. Changing the active model or provider recalculates price availability.

The focused run across subagent execution, cost reporting, agent accounting, aggregate budgets, and workflow management completed with **126 pass, 0 fail**, 371 assertions. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. The complete `bun test tests` run then completed with **2811 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8510 assertions across 250 files (196.98 s). The same six host/opt-in skips apply. No model call or eval ran.

This avoids unsupported provider-cost estimates and enforces configured dollar limits only where a verified rate exists. Provider invoices, proxy billing, lost-response billing, and cost attribution for requests outside the audited agent/subagent paths remain unchecked.

### External authorization-policy edits take effect at tool boundaries — 2026-10-16

The agent reloads and validates effective permission, risk, workflow, and authorization-hook settings before evaluating a tool and again at the final journal executor boundary. Standalone workflow starts reload policy before consent and after the authorization prompt. If a policy changes during approval, execution stops and the action must be authorized again. A detected policy change clears session tool/directory approvals and additional-directory grants, records session-grant revocations, and updates settings used by future orchestrator and workflow runs. Unreadable settings and malformed authorization rules fail closed.

Regressions cover a new deny before authorization, a deny written while the tool is queued at the executor boundary, invalid JSON, propagation into orchestrator settings, and standalone workflows disabled before or during consent. The combined focus across `tests/agent-authorization.test.ts` and `tests/agent-evidence-compaction.test.ts` completed with **33 pass, 0 fail**, 176 assertions. A corrected resume test now waits for workspace reconciliation and checks omitted-reference counts without assuming a fixed number. After adding cancellation of active delegated work and workflows on a detected policy change, the complete `bun test tests` run completed with **2816 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8531 assertions across 250 files (196.76 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` passed. No model call or eval ran.

This is a reread barrier, not an atomic lock against a settings writer that edits policy after the final reread. Settings detection is boundary-triggered; separately owned agents have to reach their own boundary, and cancellation cannot reverse an external effect that has already started or escaped process supervision.

### Active work stops when an authorization change is observed — 2026-10-16

When an Agent observes a changed authorization policy, it updates the primary orchestrator and workflow manager, cancels active tasks in its registry, and waits for active workflows to shut down. The regression holds one delegated task and one workflow agent call open, changes the external settings file, and verifies both runs end as cancelled with the policy-change reason. The same cancellation path runs when `applySettings` receives a changed authorization policy.

`bun test tests/agent-authorization.test.ts` completed with **32 pass, 0 fail**, 152 assertions. The complete `bun test tests` suite completed with **2816 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8531 assertions across 250 files (196.76 s). `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran.

Cancellation begins only when that Agent reads the new policy at an authorization boundary or through `applySettings`. The filesystem reread is not an atomic lock against later edits, and cancellation cannot undo an external effect already performed or an unregistered process that escaped supervision.

### Static review of benchmark checkers — 2026-10-16

All 28 executable task checkers present on 2026-10-16 were read alongside their task prompts and visible tests; none was executed. Seven checkers had weak “add tests” checks that searched raw test-file text for a keyword. They now ignore comments and test names, inspect runnable test bodies, and require assertions tied to the requested operation or behavior. The subsequently added `tool-discovery-relevance` checker has deterministic fixture coverage documented below but was outside that 28-checker static pass. The behavioral expectations and thresholds were not changed. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed after these edits.

This is a local static review, not a separate reviewer’s audit. Hidden checks and model eval remain unexecuted at Marcelo’s request, so no task pass rate or causal quality result is claimed.

### Explicit termination request for a reattached Linux process group — 2026-10-16

Linux process identities now persist the session ID in addition to boot ID, PID namespace, PID and start time. After the leader exits, the observer counts only live `/proc` entries that match both the recorded process-group ID and session ID. `/task <id> cancel` sends SIGTERM when a fresh observation confirms that group is still live, including a same-session descendant-only group. It rechecks liveness for up to one second, escalates to SIGKILL if the same group remains active, then observes for up to another half-second. Snapshots without a session ID remain readable and monitorable but do not authorize signaling. The task remains failed with its `INTERRUPTED` result, so stopping a process cannot be mistaken for effect reconciliation; resume remains blocked.

The regressions restore tasks into fresh registries, terminate a live group both before and after its original leader exits, verify SIGKILL escalation for a descendant that ignores SIGTERM, observe the group exit, reject a tampered session ID, accept legacy snapshots without a session ID, and confirm the interrupted result still cannot resume. The focused process run completed with **9 pass, 0 fail**, 83 assertions. The full deterministic `bun test tests` run completed with **2817 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8546 assertions across 250 files (196.34 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript LSP fixture, three OAuth streaming cases, the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed after the latest change. No model call or eval ran.

The `/proc` observation and group signal are not atomic; the process group can theoretically change between them. Escalation is bounded and best-effort: after the final observation, a process that remains active is reported rather than treated as stopped. Eligible Linux Shell stdout/stderr journal paging after controller restart is covered by the later stream-journal update. If the supervisor dies mid-run, only complete records already present remain readable; the journal flags incomplete/`gapUnknown`, and anonymous pipes cannot be reassociated or the command replayed. Escaped/unregistered process discovery, external-effect reconciliation, and non-Linux validation remain open.

### Background Shell supervisor and live-preview reattachment — 2026-10-16

Eligible Linux background Shell tasks now run under a detached supervisor that remains alive after the CLI controller exits. Task restoration validates the same session, task fingerprint, and Linux process identity, then reattaches its runner to that supervisor; it never starts the command again. The supervisor atomically persists a private, bounded state file with secret-masked output previews, which the reattached runner publishes through the task's partial status. A separate bounded journal now stores already-redacted stdout/stderr records for cursor paging as documented in the 2026-10-18 update. On completion, the task recovers the existing redacted output artifact and workspace observation.

The integration regression launches the original CLI controller as a separate process, waits until the supervisor is attachable, persists a running task snapshot, and exits that controller. A restored session sees the live preview, receives the final output, and verifies a filesystem marker was written once. A supervisor regression checks preview redaction and output recovery across the controller exit. The focused run across Shell background, supervisor, streaming capture and output privacy completed with **34 pass, 1 skip, 0 fail**, 169 assertions; the skip is the unsupported-sandbox case on this Linux host. The complete deterministic `bun test tests` run completed with **2820 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8565 assertions across 251 files (202.77 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported-sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran.

This closes runner and bounded live-preview reattachment for eligible Linux background Shell tasks only. It does not reconnect arbitrary task runners, discover unregistered or escaped processes, reconcile network/database/external effects, or validate macOS/Windows process supervision. A command containing inline credentials may have its saved metadata redacted so its fingerprint no longer matches; restoration then fails closed and does not replay it. Model-quality and causal-effect claims still require the cancelled evals.

### Fork workers require explicit bounded dependencies — 2026-10-16

Fork-mode workers now receive only prior results whose task IDs are explicitly listed in `dependencies`, serialized in the declared order. Empty, omitted or unmatched dependencies contribute no prior context. The complete JSON payload is capped at 16,000 UTF-8 bytes; it stops before adding a result that would exceed the cap, preserving complete records. The `subagent` schema documents this behavior. Included results remain untrusted claims that workers must verify.

`bun test tests/orchestration-fork-context.test.ts` completed with **3 pass, 0 fail**, 10 assertions. Regressions cover explicit filtering, dependency order, empty/missing dependencies, per-reference bounds and the total UTF-8 cap. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. The complete test suite was not rerun. This narrows context by declared dependency and bounds payload size; it does not establish improved worker quality or token savings. E11/E12 model comparisons remain unexecuted at Marcelo's request.

### Tool-call argument batches receive an independent byte cap — 2026-10-16

All native streaming, native non-streaming and Bedrock prompt-based tool-call paths now pre-parse each response batch against a 40,000-byte UTF-8 argument-payload limit. The first call that exceeds the remaining budget and all later calls in that batch are refused before execution. Their raw arguments are replaced with `{}` in retained native tool-call messages; rejected Bedrock call blocks are replaced with a fixed omission marker. Calls earlier in the batch may already have run, so the guard does not roll back their effects. Provider response text is already allocated before this boundary, and tool arguments and tool results still have separate caps rather than a shared combined budget.

`bun test tests/harness-feedback.test.ts` completed with **9 pass, 0 fail**, 42 assertions. `bun test tests/streaming.test.ts` completed with **12 pass, 3 skip, 0 fail**, 36 assertions; the skips are pre-existing OAuth-only cases. The integration regression verifies an in-budget write can proceed while the crossing and later calls do not execute and their payloads are absent from retained history. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. The full deterministic suite has not yet been rerun after this change. No model call or eval ran.

The native `role: tool` path now shares one 40,000-byte UTF-8 budget between accepted assistant tool-call argument strings and their serialized result envelopes. The same bounding path applies when the primary agent or a delegated worker prepares API messages.

### Private secondary disk recovery for Shell artifacts — 2026-10-16

When primary Shell artifact storage fails, the writer now tries a separate private recovery directory before using the process-local memory cache. Both disk locations enforce the existing per-session limits of 10 artifacts/20 MiB, use directories with mode 0700 and files with mode 0600, and store the same secret-redacted bounded content and provenance. The artifact metadata and read headers identify recovery-disk storage and report the primary write failure. Readers, provenance lookup, task reattachment, and the detached supervisor use the same fallback location; command execution is never repeated to recover output.

The new regression forces the primary path to fail, verifies recovery-disk creation, private file permissions, redaction, content paging, and provenance recovery. The focused privacy test passed with **6 pass, 0 fail**, 39 assertions. The complete suite result above predates this secondary-storage change; broader Shell integration and the suite still need to run. If both disk locations fail, behavior falls back to bounded process memory and can still lose recovery on process exit or quota eviction. No model call or eval ran.

### Grapheme-safe Unicode cuts across reads, captures and tool budgets — 2026-10-16

UTF-16 reads, shell output paging, shell capture/storage limits, bounded previews, shell summaries, and native/Bedrock result truncation now preserve extended grapheme clusters. `ReadFile` byte ranges identify grapheme boundaries during the existing streaming hash/UTF-8 scan, retain only the unfinished trailing cluster, reject `start_byte` values inside a cluster, and stop at the preceding safe boundary when the requested byte cap falls inside one. Look-behind is bounded to 16,000 bytes; a cluster that cannot fit a byte slice or be validated within that bound is refused instead of split. Preview capture holds the trailing cluster across stream chunks; a single cluster larger than the preview line bound disables that channel's live preview with an explicit notice while archived capture remains independently bounded.

The complete deterministic `bun test tests` run completed with **2842 pass, 6 skip, 0 fail**, 8665 assertions across 252 files (209.35 s). The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript server, three OAuth tool-truncation cases, unsupported-sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only launcher. No model call or eval ran. The saved session goal now appears in a separate project-context section after each context refresh, including compaction and resume. The criteria preview now carries each criterion's source ID and a bounded exact quote excerpt so an older source can be recovered with `session_evidence`. The packet explicitly gives later user requests and corrections precedence and does not treat the goal as completion evidence or permission. ### Primary session checkpoints and interrupted-tool recovery — 2026-10-16

The primary TUI now persists the active session after authorization and immediately before a tool effect. The checkpoint is secret-redacted and atomically replaces the session file using a private temporary file; unlike the best-effort UI save, a checkpoint write failure is returned to the tool boundary and prevents the effect from starting. On restore, native tool calls with missing results and Bedrock prompt calls with missing result envelopes receive synthetic unknown-outcome results. The session context and TUI warn that effects may have happened and must be inspected before any retry; the tools are never replayed by session loading.

The six skips were the opt-in real TypeScript LSP fixture, three OAuth streaming cases, unsupported-sandbox coverage on this Linux host, and the Windows-only browser launcher. No model call or eval ran.

The recovery record does not establish whether an interrupted external effect happened; it prevents blind replay and leaves reconciliation to the current integration and user. The TUI checkpoint wiring is not exercised by a dedicated kill-and-restart UI integration fixture. Its file is synced before rename and the containing directory after rename on platforms where Node supports directory sync; Windows still lacks a portable directory-sync path, so power-loss durability is not universal. The research implementation goal remains active.

### Bounded Shell file-change receipts and checkpoint flush — 2026-10-16

Uninterrupted background Shell executions now retain per-file source-input digests long enough to report added, modified, and removed paths in the task partial. The persisted receipt is capped at 100 paths and reports the exact omitted count. A restored runner without the original in-memory manifest keeps the aggregate digest comparison where available and marks file-level detail unknown. This improves local workspace evidence only; `effects: not-reconciled` remains in place for databases, network calls, and other external effects.

The pre-effect TUI session checkpoint now syncs its private temporary file before atomic rename and syncs the containing directory on supported non-Windows platforms. It also syncs parent directories created for a new session path where directory sync is supported. Ordinary best-effort session saves keep their existing lower-cost path. Windows syncs the file but has no portable Node directory-sync path, so rename persistence across power loss remains platform-dependent.

### New-file writes cannot replace a racing create — 2026-10-17

`write_file` now binds an unversioned existing-file replacement to the raw bytes read for its diff and rechecks the hash after staging, before publication. For a missing target it carries an explicit “missing” version; `atomicWriteFile` stages the contents in the target directory and publishes with `link`, whose creation fails atomically if another writer has occupied the destination. The focused regressions inject an external update during staging and an external create at the exact publication boundary, verify that external bytes survive, and also cover a normal successful create. Filesystems that do not support same-directory hard links fail closed. There remains a final check-to-rename race for replacement of existing files and a check-to-unlink race for deletion.

`bun test tests/file-commit-cancellation.test.ts tests/completionGates.test.ts tests/fileCheckpoint.test.ts` completed with **38 pass, 0 fail**, 126 assertions. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. No model call or eval ran. Existing-target final races, deletion races, complete external-effect reconciliation, and the other P0 gaps remain open.

### Core edit tools bind commits to the snapshot they transformed — 2026-10-17

`edit_file` line edits and `patch_file` now hash the exact source bytes they read and pass that version to `atomicWriteFile`; an earlier runtime read version still takes precedence. `write_file` already passed its raw-byte snapshot. `atomicWriteFile` now refuses an unversioned commit, so all file-tool callers must provide a version precondition. Regressions inject an update during staging for both tools and confirm the competing bytes survive. The cooperating cross-process lease serializes app writers, which then recheck the source snapshot after acquiring the lease. A non-cooperating external process can still race the final check-to-rename or check-to-unlink window.

### Safe bot previews journal Shell proposals without running effect-freshness checks — 2026-10-17

In `simulationMode`, Shell and Git proposals are intercepted by the bot's durable simulation journal and never execute. The agent now skips workspace/execution-version capture in this explicit no-effect mode so a proposal is still recorded as simulated. Normal sessions retain the existing snapshot checks and final executor-boundary revalidation. ### Runtime probes and paginated grep results — 2026-10-17

The system environment block now reports the active Bun/Node runtime, known lockfiles at the Git root, the package manager declared in a bounded `package.json` or inferred only from a single lockfile manager, and the package's declared `engines`. It does not include package contents; metadata reads stop at 64 KiB. If multiple manager families have lockfiles and no declaration, the prompt reports ambiguity. A fixed root filename allowlist also identifies Python, Rust, Go, Ruby, PHP, Maven, Gradle, and Deno configuration manifests without reading their contents. Nested/target-specific manifests and executable identity remain unreported.

`grep` accepts a bounded page size and offset, groups results by file, prints an explicit continuation when more matches remain, and streams native grep output instead of buffering the full subprocess result. Both native and JavaScript paths truncate individual long lines at grapheme boundaries and cap retained page content at 50,000 UTF-8 bytes; the native reader continues counting matches after the display cap so its continuation offset remains usable. The test exposed that GNU `-z` treated the whole file as one record; the native path now uses `-Z` for NUL-safe filenames and parses newline-delimited matches, preserving line numbers. M14 remains partial because grouping is not symbol-aware and continuation is not bound to a workspace/query snapshot.

`bun test tests/grep-pagination.test.ts tests/platform.test.ts tests/schema-freshness.test.ts tests/tools.test.ts` completed with **56 pass, 0 fail**, 429 assertions. The 200-match large-line regression verifies bounded grouped output and working continuation on native and JavaScript paths. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed after the implementation. The full suite has not been rerun after this grep change. The preceding complete `bun test tests` run passed with **2,891 pass, 5 skip, 0 fail**, 8,870 assertions across 259 files (217.80 s); its first aggregate run had one load-sensitive detached Shell-supervisor reattachment failure (**2,890 pass, 5 skip, 1 fail**), which passed alone and on the full repeat. The five skips remain the real TypeScript LSP integration, three OAuth-only streaming tests, and the Windows-only browser launcher. E01–E20 were not run.

Recursive grep now excludes sensitive filename patterns and AWS configuration directories in native grep before it reads files; native capability detection confirms support for those exclusion flags. The JavaScript fallback rejects the same sensitive paths and uses `lstat` so a non-sensitive symlink alias cannot expose a protected target. A case-insensitive path regression covers `.env`, `.aws/config`, and a symlink alias in both paths. The focused run across grep, path safety, tool schemas, environment context, and platform helpers completed with **105 pass, 0 fail**, 554 assertions. The subsequent complete deterministic `bun test tests` run completed with **2,894 pass, 5 skip, 0 fail**, 8,888 assertions across 259 files (214.66 s). The skips were the opt-in real TypeScript LSP integration, three OAuth-only streaming tests, and the Windows-only browser launcher. Typecheck, build, and diff-check passed. No model eval ran.

A follow-up regression also searches directly from `.aws` and supplies `include: '.env'`. GNU grep applies the last matching include/exclude rule, so model-controlled includes now precede the safety exclusions; this prevents an explicit include from overriding them. The focused six-file run completed with **105 pass, 0 fail**, 558 assertions. The complete deterministic suite above predates this argument-order correction. No model eval ran.

### Grep continuation is bound to its query and matching-result snapshot — 2026-10-17

Paged grep now returns bounded, single-use in-memory cursors bound to the session, workspace, search directory, pattern, include filter, page size, protected paths, and complete matching-result hash. A continuation reruns the search and is rejected if the query or matching results changed. Native grep hashes streamed output; the JavaScript fallback hashes every matching file, line number, and full line. Cursors expire after ten minutes and are lost when the process restarts. Language-aware symbol grouping remains open.

### Grep can group matches by configured LSP symbols — 2026-10-17

`grep` now accepts `group_by: "symbol"`. It requests document symbols only from an already configured language server, assigns each match to the narrowest enclosing nested symbol, sanitizes server-provided labels, and preserves file-level output for unsupported files or failed lookups with an explicit notice. At most four matching files are queried per page; the cursor binds the grouping mode and LSP configuration. Default file grouping starts no language-server process.

### Delegated tasks stop automatic retries after possible tool effects — 2026-10-17

Before an authorized delegated tool can execute, the task records `effectMayHaveStarted` and emits an `effect_started` event. The marker is included in snapshots, runtime prompt context, and `/tasks`, and prevents automatic retries after a later retryable failure. Only core local read tools are exempt; unknown and discovered tools fail closed. Task resume is refused while the marker remains, so the caller must inspect evidence, reconcile effects, and start a new authorized task. This closes blind automatic replay for the delegated executor, but does not add idempotency keys or reconcile remote outcomes.

`bun test tests/task-registry.test.ts tests/subagent-executor.test.ts tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts tests/schema-freshness.test.ts tests/agent.test.ts` completed with **128 pass, 0 fail**, 390 assertions. Regressions verify the retry guard, refusal to resume a marked task, delegated authorization boundary, marker persistence on interrupted-session restore, and task/prompt visibility. The focused typecheck, build, and `git diff --check` passed. Build output also follows the project-wide English convention. No model call or eval ran.

### Unknown task effects are persisted before runner entry — 2026-10-17

Task records now carry an optional effect policy for snapshot compatibility. New runners default to `unknown`; with session snapshots enabled, the possible-effect marker is flushed before arbitrary runner code starts. Snapshot failure prevents runner entry, and cancellation during a pending marker flush also prevents entry and releases the scheduler slot. Delegated subagents declare `tool-gated` and persist the marker immediately before authorized non-read or unclassified tool execution. Only explicitly `retry-safe` runners bypass the upfront marker. Older snapshots without a policy restore as `unknown`. Marked tasks still require reconciliation before retry or resume; the system does not prove that a `tool-gated` or `retry-safe` declaration is truthful and does not reconcile remote effects.

Managed task workspace acquisition now propagates cancellation into the `WorktreeCreate` hook and Git subprocesses; cancellation cannot fall back to an in-place writer, and an interrupted add only attempts cleanup of its generated workspace path. The POSIX regression proves the delayed hook side effect does not run after cancellation.

The focused run `bun test tests/task-registry.test.ts tests/subagent-executor.test.ts tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts tests/schema-freshness.test.ts tests/agent.test.ts tests/orchestration-workspace.test.ts tests/subagent-git-authorization.test.ts` completed with **166 pass, 0 fail**, 547 assertions. The tests include marker flush ordering/failure/cancellation, legacy snapshots without a policy, worker retry/resume behavior, task workspace cancellation, and Git authorization. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, the website production build, and `git diff --check` passed. The complete deterministic suite was not rerun after these changes. E01–E20 remain canceled; no model call or eval ran.

Grep stores bounded, single-use cursor state in a private per-session file under `~/.deepseek/grep-cursors`. When private storage cannot be used, the tool identifies the cursor as process-local instead of implying restart recovery.

### Git mutation failures report uncertain local effects — 2026-10-17

Git `add`, `commit`, branch creation/switch (`checkout`), and `stash` failures now return `GIT_LOCAL_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` when the invoked command exits nonzero or output capture is incomplete. The error is non-retryable and directs the caller to inspect and reconcile the repository before retrying. Authorization and configuration checks that fail before the Git mutation starts retain their existing refusal behavior. This prevents blind retries but does not reconcile the index, worktree, refs, or stash. Regressions cover a failed `add`, stash-pop merge conflicts with unmerged index entries, and configured filter/merge-program refusals.

`bun test tests/git-local-mutations.test.ts tests/git-remote-execution.test.ts tests/git-pull-programs.test.ts tests/git-content-filters.test.ts tests/git-inspection.test.ts` passed with **36 pass, 0 fail**, 150 assertions. `bun run typecheck` and `git diff --check` passed. ### Emergency temporary-disk recovery for truncated Shell output — 2026-10-17

When either home-based Shell output store fails, saving now also attempts a redacted, checksummed copy beneath a private per-user temporary-directory root. Reads, provenance lookup, background task reattachment, and detached supervisors use the same emergency location. Retention is bounded to 10 artifacts/20 MiB per session, with 0700 directories and 0600 artifact files. The fallback improves recovery when a home-based location fails but is not durable across reboot or temporary-directory cleanup, is not guaranteed to use a separate physical device, and still falls back to process memory or reports failure if all disk stores fail. A recovery-path alias regression ensures the emergency write is attempted even when the configured recovery path is the same failed directory as the primary.

`bun test tests/shell-output-privacy.test.ts tests/shell-stream-capture.test.ts tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts tests/shell-supervisor.test.ts` passed with **45 pass, 0 fail**, 227 assertions. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. The complete `bun test tests --parallel=2 --max-concurrency=4` suite passed with **2,930 pass, 4 skip, 0 fail**, 9,114 assertions across 262 files (157.71 s). E01–E20 remain canceled; no model call or eval ran.

It returns a typed partial envelope with the process state, exit code or signal, a grapheme-safe 1,000-unit output preview, incompleteness/truncation flags, and the retained output ID when available. The primary Agent can therefore distinguish a failed command from ordinary text and read its retained output without rerunning it. Other composite outputs and unrecognized legacy failure strings remain outside this exact-format normalizer.

### Explicit user reconciliation for interrupted task effects — 2026-10-17

Task control now durably records an attempt-bound user review (`no-effect`, `effect-observed`, or `compensated`). A stopped, settled task can resume only after a `no-effect` review for that exact attempt; the review requires nonempty evidence, rejects recognized credentials, and is rolled back in memory if its snapshot write fails. This is a human assertion, not integration-specific verification of Git, network, database, or other external state. Automatic reconciliation remains open.

`bun test tests/task-registry.test.ts tests/commands.test.ts tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts` passed with **82 pass, 0 fail**, 224 assertions. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. A subsequent serial full-suite process ended after its output/exit-status channel was lost; its result is unconfirmed and is not counted as a passing run. The previous complete deterministic suite above remains the latest confirmed full-suite result. E01–E20 remain canceled; no model call or eval ran.

### Delegated local Git effect reconciliation — 2026-10-17

Delegated local Git `add`, `checkout`, `commit`, and `stash` failures now use the attempt-bound effect ledger when available. The Git integration hashes bounded before/after snapshots containing HEAD, branch, staged entries, refs, porcelain status, tracked working-tree diff, and effective configuration. An unchanged snapshot can be durably recorded as `no-effect`; a changed snapshot is recorded as `effect-observed`; incomplete inspection stays `unknown`. Git diff contents and configuration values are hashed in memory and are not returned. This is limited to failed local Git commands with an effect ID and durable task storage. It does not reconcile remote Git, Shell/network/database effects, arbitrary other tools, or external-writer races; equal snapshots are not causal proof if another writer changes and restores state between observations.

`bun test tests/task-registry.test.ts tests/orchestration-persistence.test.ts tests/subagent-executor.test.ts tests/git-local-mutations.test.ts tests/git-inspection.test.ts tests/git-content-filters.test.ts` passed with **111 pass, 0 fail**, 403 assertions. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed after the final changes. No complete suite or model eval ran after this change; E01–E20 remain canceled.

The delegated executor now treats Git `status`, `diff`, `log`, branch listing, and batches containing only those read actions as retry-safe. Reading saved Shell output through `output_id` is also retry-safe. Git mutations and unknown actions remain effect-marked immediately before execution. The regression confirms the read calls receive no effect ID while `git add` still receives one.

`OrchestratorSession` now supplies the task registry with an effect-marker persistence callback only when a snapshot store exists. A session configured without snapshots can no longer pass a no-op callback that falsely makes an integration-verified `no-effect` outcome eligible for automatic task resume. The ledger remains fail-closed when durable storage is absent.

### Interrupted tasks without process identity remain non-resumable — 2026-10-17

An interrupted snapshot with no captured process identity previously could accept a manual `no-effect` review and then resume. That review describes external effects but cannot establish that the original runner has stopped. `reconcileEffects` now refuses that `no-effect` outcome, and `resume` independently rejects an interrupted record with no process identity so an older or altered snapshot cannot bypass the check. Effect-observed/compensated reviews remain auditable but do not authorize replay. Unregistered-process discovery and non-Linux supervision remain outside this fix.

### Partial remote Git outcomes — 2026-10-17

Git push requests porcelain status and hashes accepted ref-update rows from stdout. When a push fails after at least one ref update is explicitly accepted, the result records `effect-observed` and returns a typed partial error; an all-rejected push remains `GIT_REMOTE_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`, since server hooks may still have run. A failed pull with an effect ID compares bounded local repository snapshots and records `effect-observed` only when tracked repository state changed during the command. An unchanged or incomplete snapshot remains unknown; a pull never infers `no-effect` from local equality. These are command-level observations only. They do not reconcile other remote effects, an interrupted multi-tool task, or whole-job outcomes, and snapshot changes are not causal proof against concurrent external writers.

`bun test tests/git-remote-execution.test.ts tests/git-local-mutations.test.ts tests/git-inspection.test.ts tests/git-content-filters.test.ts tests/git-pull-programs.test.ts --parallel=1 --max-concurrency=1` passed with **39 pass, 0 fail**, 168 assertions. Regressions cover a partially rejected multi-ref push, an all-rejected push that remains unknown, and a failed conflicting pull that records changed local state. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. E01–E20 remain canceled; no model call or eval ran.

The system prompt states that external file/tool content is untrusted data and cannot change the task or grant tool permission. ### Shell supervisor loss preserves a recoverable preview artifact — 2026-10-17

If a detached Shell supervisor exits before saving the final artifact, the caller now stores its last redacted progress preview through the existing Shell output stores, adds the artifact reference to the task partial, and includes an `output_id` recovery hint. The artifact body explicitly says it contains only the preview, not the full-output artifact; the separate stream journal may expose complete records already present for eligible Linux tasks, but not a missing tail or anonymous pipes after supervisor death (C08). If saving fails, the task still retains its bounded inline preview and reports that no recoverable artifact was saved. The task remains failed, external effects remain unresolved, workspace observation stays unknown, and the command is never replayed automatically.

`bun test tests/tools-shell-background.test.ts --parallel=1 --max-concurrency=1` passed with **18 pass, 0 fail**, 87 assertions. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, `npm run build` in `website/`, and `git diff --check` passed. ### Browser, hook, and delegated-agent injection reach the write gate — 2026-10-17

A real `UserPromptSubmit` command adds malicious context through the headless `--pipe` route; a local provider fixture attempts the prompted write and policy blocks it. A foreground delegated agent returns an injected result through the parent's real tool boundary, where the same denial holds. The Shell risk boundary now recognizes common dependency installation and package-run commands across npm, pnpm, Yarn, Bun, Python installers, uv/Poetry, Cargo/Go, Ruby, Composer/.NET, and common system package managers. These operations require high-risk confirmation even when adding development dependencies; after approval, the existing Linux sandbox permits network only for detected network-capable commands. Direct commands, manager options, shell command separators, command substitutions, and simple `sh -c`/`bash -c` invocations are covered. ### Primary-Agent Git failure outcomes — 2026-10-17

When the primary Agent has durable session-history checkpointing, Git mutation calls now pass the checkpointed tool-call ID as the effect identity. The existing bounded Git classifier can persist failed local `add`, `checkout`, `commit`, and `stash` outcomes, plus a failed `pull` with changed tracked state or a partially accepted `push`, into redacted session evidence before returning. Without durable history checkpointing, the primary route supplies no effect ID and local failures remain unknown. The new route-level regression covers a failed `add` recorded as `no-effect`; local Git tests cover the underlying bounded classifier. This does not reconcile an interrupted command after process loss, concurrent external-writer races, or a whole multi-tool turn.

### Primary-Agent foreground Shell workspace evidence — 2026-10-17

The primary Agent now passes the checkpointed tool-call ID to foreground Shell execution when durable session-history checkpointing is configured. The existing bounded workspace observer records `effect-observed` in redacted session evidence only when the process group settles and source-input workspace state changes. ### Primary-Agent bounded turn-effect summary — 2026-10-17

Primary Agent turns with durable history checkpointing write a bounded reconciliation summary on normal completion and reconstruct one for interrupted turns before resume. Recovery matches each saved attempt to the exact transcript call ID and tool, and accepts only supported bounded Git or file receipts tied to that call; everything else stays unknown. It blocks resume if it cannot durably save the reconstructed summary, and more than 128 attempts forces manual review. Shell/network/database, hook, MCP, unmatched, omitted, and other unclassified effects remain unknown; this is not whole-job reconciliation.

### Additional fail-closed platform and MCP boundaries — 2026-10-17

A deterministic subprocess regression sets the platform to Darwin and confirms that foreground Shell, foreground verification, read-only verification, and background Shell routes do not execute without Linux bubblewrap. The test checks unsupported-platform control flow; it does not emulate native macOS/Windows containment. POSIX process-group cancellation now signals the owned direct child if the expected process group is already absent, covering the case where the leader exited before a descendant.

Approved stdio MCP servers now launch inside Linux bubblewrap with a read-only workspace, isolated network/process namespaces, sanitized runtime environment, and parent-bound process cleanup. This contains project writes and host network access, but intentionally breaks stdio servers that need network, writable project state, downloads, or package caches outside mounted locations; HTTP MCP is unchanged. This is a restrictive safety boundary, not granular per-server network/write capabilities.
