### Typed cancellation, artifact leases, and interrupted-call replay guard — 2026-10-17

Closing `ask_user_questions` with `cancelled:true` is now normalized to a typed `TOOL_CANCELLED` partial result, preserving any answers collected before the user closed the prompt. A successful `cancelled:false` JSON result is unchanged. C20 artifact saves, pruning, and supervisor reservations now share a per-session local-host lease; deterministic contention coverage synchronizes on an actual failed lease acquisition, and a quota regression proves that a supervisor's `reservedOutputId` stays pinned before the artifact is published. This coordination remains local-host only, and a live or reused PID can leave a stale lease failing closed until its bounded wait expires. If removal fails after a lease directory is renamed to its unique quarantine name, the lease is still released but a private temporary directory may remain; no automatic orphan-quarantine cleanup is implemented.

Cloud-mutation warnings now distinguish missing literal provider context (which may rely on ambient identity/account/region/cluster settings) from an explicit selector or provider-qualified resource ID. Neither check verifies the effective identity and destination or verifies/binds a deployment plan to approval. The Shell risk scanner ignores comment text and non-expanding heredoc bodies while continuing to inspect executable command expansions inside unquoted heredocs. It remains a heuristic parser with unsupported shell grammar and opaque arbitrary launchers.

A restored primary tool call matching a persisted interrupted-call digest now requires fresh one-call approval; existing session grants and auto-approval do not authorize it. After asynchronous authorization refresh, execution rechecks cancellation, objective revision, current authorization, the applicable Shell/Git workspace fingerprint, and project instructions. The replay marker clears only when the result checkpoint is saved and is restored if that save fails. This narrows one replay path but does not reconcile arbitrary, network, database, or otherwise unclassified effects.

The focused integrated run passed **109 tests, 0 failures**, with 497 assertions across six files, and `bun run typecheck` passed. A separate C57 follow-up began after that run; therefore these results do not validate its final diff. The full deterministic suite and production build have not yet been rerun for the current changes. E01–E20 remain canceled; no provider call or model eval ran.


### Delegated file-write effect receipts — 2026-10-18

`atomicWriteFile` now emits a positive task effect outcome only after publishing the file and awaiting the existing file-commit callback, and only when the execution context carries a delegated task ID, effect ID, and outcome recorder. Evidence contains SHA-256 digests for the canonical path and written bytes; the primary Agent continues to use its stronger checkpoint-bound receipt. A failed receipt write returns a typed partial result that says the file was committed and must be inspected, while failures before publication remain unknown. This covers the shared native write/edit/patch path, not arbitrary filesystem effects or whole-job reconciliation.

`bun test tests/file-commit-cancellation.test.ts --parallel=1 --max-concurrency=1` passed with **13 tests, 0 failures**, 57 assertions. The regression checks commit-before-outcome ordering, no outcome on a stale precondition, unchanged primary-route behavior, and explicit partial status when receipt persistence fails. No model call, eval, provider, or external service ran.

### Lifecycle journal integrity, typed memory failures, and final validation — 2026-10-18

Lifecycle recovery now detects malformed entries under the primary-agent hook marker prefix. It writes a separate metadata-only `primary_agent_hook_effect_recovery_v1` record with the malformed evidence ID, unknown event/state, and manual-review flag; it never copies the untrusted marker payload. Resume includes an unknown-effect warning, does not replay the hook, and is blocked if the recovery record cannot be checkpointed. This remains limited to the eight journaled lifecycle events, including `CwdChanged`, and durable-history sessions; other lifecycle hooks remain outside the journal, and hooks without checkpointing remain best-effort.

C57 package-script detection now covers custom-name direct shorthands for pnpm, Yarn, and Bun, including Corepack pnpm/Yarn. Known built-ins are excluded, unknown manager options fail closed, and a package-script approval can be retained only for the same command arguments and workspace version. Script execution alone does not grant network access. Path-like pnpm/Yarn shorthands with slash or backslash require high-risk approval, and Windows drive-path manager executables are preserved through tokenization before classification. Direct Bun source-file commands remain outside package-script classification. Arbitrary launchers, a full shell grammar, a lockfile/script preview, and workspace-secret masking remain unsupported or unverified; approved scripts can still read local secrets and change writable workspace files.

Memory mutation failures and no-ops now return typed envelopes with stable codes for denied consent, invalid or unsafe content, disabled storage, missing or ambiguous matches, duplicates, and capacity limits. Successful writes and list/review output are unchanged. Tests cover no-mutation behavior and fail-closed outcomes. The source-level memory API keeps its existing string contract.

The input tests now cover exact bracketed-paste round-trip through a long-paste placeholder and submit, including Unicode, indentation, blank lines, and trailing whitespace. Selection coverage verifies that soft-wrapped rows reconstruct into logical source lines while hard newlines remain.

### Shared primary-turn token and cost budgets — 2026-10-18

Each primary turn receives a budget-group ID. When `agents.maxAggregateTokens` or `agents.maxAggregateCostUsd` is configured, primary provider requests and in-turn prompt refinement acquire a serialized lease; delegated workers inherit the same group, and MoA requests charge measured usage to it. The request output limit is capped before dispatch where possible. After a response, reported usage is added to the group; missing usage or an exceeded budget blocks later requests, and an over-limit tool response is not executed. Cost-limited calls require verified pricing. The Settings UI now describes the budget as spanning primary and delegated calls, and warns that prompt-token/cost overshoot is detected after dispatch. The implementation work reported 44 focused deterministic budget tests passing; typecheck and diff-check passed. No provider call or model eval ran.

This is a configured best-effort budget, not a hard ceiling on provider usage or billing. Prompt tokens/cost are not reserved before dispatch, so one response can exceed the remaining total before the excess is known; a provider ignoring `max_tokens` can also overshoot. The shared group ledger is in memory; task records can seed restored worker usage, but primary usage is not persisted in a task record. At the time of this entry, byte limits were per operation and repeated operations could exceed a cumulative turn or worker-tree byte quota; the current C21 and M29 crosswalks and P0 summary supersede that statement. E01–E20 remain canceled at Marcelo's request; no quality, causal billing, or provider-readiness claim is made.

Late primary background SubAgent outcomes now have a durable same-process reconciliation path. The detached monitor waits for the exact task subtree to become terminal and leave `inFlight`, then waits for effect propagation before sending an aggregate receipt. TaskRegistry propagation is serialized per top-level task tree, so unrelated task roots do not delay this barrier. The primary Agent accepts only a positive receipt bound to the exact checkpointed primary tool-call ID, root child metadata, settled descendant aggregate, and unique still-unknown non-hooked `subagent` attempt; it appends a new summary version and checkpoints it. Ambiguous identities, absent/malformed receipts, no positive effect, and checkpoint failure remain unknown. Interrupted-turn recovery can also adopt a late receipt only when that receipt was durably checkpointed and the restored child task and aggregate still match. If the child settles but no primary receipt checkpoint survives the crash, recovery leaves the attempt unknown rather than reconstructing an outcome from an incomplete record; universal whole-job reconciliation remains open.

### Linux detached Shell stream journal — 2026-10-18

Eligible Linux detached Shell tasks now expose already-redacted stdout/stderr records through `stream_task_id` and `stream_cursor`. The private append-only journal is bounded to 2 MB per task and 20 MB/10 journals per session; page access expires after seven days, quota pressure can prune inactive journals sooner, and each page is capped at 20,000 characters. Pages preserve sequence and channel offsets and report known gaps, truncation, conservative redaction, expiry, incomplete tails, and unknown gaps. Reservation/storage failure can make the journal unavailable, while quota or queue pressure can truncate records. After a controller/CLI restart, callers can page persisted records; task restoration can also reattach to the same live supervisor. If that supervisor dies mid-run, only complete records already present in the journal can be read; pages report incomplete/`gapUnknown`, anonymous pipes cannot be reassociated, and the command is never replayed. This does not add stream reattachment for arbitrary task runners or non-Linux platforms.

A regression with a completed PEM block followed by a second unterminated block on the same line failed before the final fix by exposing second-secret-line in plaintext. The scanner now tracks private-key markers in order and carries unmatched key labels across lines. ### Primary background-effect recovery after task snapshot restoration — 2026-10-18

A new integration regression now persists a settled background SubAgent child and its exact positive receipt, shuts down the original Agent, recreates the Agent from the same task snapshot, and checks that recovery accepts the receipt only while the restored child metadata and aggregate still match. Tampering with the restored child’s `primaryEffectId` keeps the reconciliation state `unknown`. This verifies snapshot restoration for this narrow SubAgent path; it does not provide whole-job reconciliation for Shell network/database effects, hooks, MCP, or other unclassified integrations. Receipt sources still use the provider `toolCallId`; reusing an ID across turns fails closed and can leave a fresh outcome unknown.

### Protected-path and cumulative-output follow-up — 2026-10-18

This current-checkout update supersedes the earlier output-coverage and full-suite statements above. Protected-path checks now compare device and inode for existing regular files after lexical and canonical-path checks, so a hard link into the workspace cannot expose a protected runtime file. Regressions cover direct resolution and ReadFolder, Glob, and Grep enumeration. This is a local filesystem check; native non-Linux behavior and model behavior were not evaluated.

The shared 16 MiB UTF-8 pool now charges complete serialized provider requests and every primary/delegated model-facing tool result or error, including custom and MCP tools. Shell avoids double charging: foreground and verification captures report raw bytes per chunk, while live/restored detached supervisors charge snapshot deltas and reconcile the final total. Detached accounting can lag by the 500 ms snapshot interval plus scheduling. Quota exhaustion skips later not-yet-started effectful calls in primary and delegated execution; already-started parallel calls settle normally. Shell capture continues after exhaustion, so the pool is not a hard process-output cutoff and cannot undo command effects. The byte ledger remains process-local.

The grouped focused run across aggregate-budget, delegated execution, Shell capture/supervision, and path-containment tests passed with **122 tests, 0 failures**, 505 assertions before the final primary guard. The final primary quota test file passed with **20 tests, 0 failures**; its two new generic-result and Shell-source regressions failed before the guard and passed afterward. The hard-link focused run passed with **65 tests, 0 failures**; Shell stream/background/supervisor tests passed with **44 tests, 0 failures**. Typecheck, package build, website production build, L1–L9 deterministic probes, and `git diff --check` passed. The website build generated the release feed and reports a **633.5 kB gzipped JavaScript bundle** (167 B above the previous reported build).

The latest full serial test run completed with **3,608 passed, 5 skipped, and 2 timed out** (3,615 total across 309 files; 12,310 assertions). The timeouts were `C25 checker validates exact bytes, requested offsets, and every path in multi-file reads` and `Agent class > askBtw > uses a separate tool-free request without changing the main history`. Each passed when rerun alone: the C25 checker in 1.8 seconds and the `askBtw` test in 1.0 second. These isolated passes do not change the recorded full-suite result; no test timeout was raised.

Still partial or unverified: exact provider-specific token preflight across heterogeneous endpoints; full artifact durability if all bounded stores fail and the process exits; the final replace/delete race with non-cooperating external writers; integration-specific reconciliation for arbitrary remote effects; native macOS/Windows process and lifecycle behavior; and model-quality claims. E01–E20 remain canceled, so no provider evaluation or causal-quality claim was made.
